# Otos Requirements

The authoritative registry of Otos's **implemented** product and system behavior. It states
current behavior only: an item's presence means Otos does this today. Future work lives in
GitHub issues, never here, and a retired requirement is deleted (git history remembers it).
Process, workflow and tooling rules live in [CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md), not here.

Every behavior change updates the affected items in the same PR. IDs are stable: ARC-*
architecture and system, APP-* product surface, ENR-* enrichment, MED-* media and originals.

Each item is one list entry, its lines contiguous:

- The first line: the ID in bold, then the requirement as an EARS sentence ("The <system> shall …", "When
  <trigger>, …", "While <state>, …", "If <condition>, then …", "Where <feature>, …"), with
  a clause list under it when the behavior has several parts. The first line ends in
  `[verified]` when tests prove it: each proving test carries a `Validates: <ID>` comment
  directly above it, so the tests point at the requirement and this file names no tests.
  Otherwise the item has an `unverified: <why>` line saying what is not machine-checked.
- `Example:` — a concrete observable example of the requirement holding.
- `Implemented in:` — the owning source files.
- `Not covered by tests:` — on a verified item, the part its tests do not reach, and why.

`script/process-check` checks that every requirement is declared once, that every
Validates tag names a declared requirement, and that `[verified]` and `unverified:` agree
with the tags. `script/requirements-authority-check.sh` checks this file holds only
current, implemented behavior, that every `Implemented in:` path exists, and that every
Validates tag sits on a test that can fail.

## Architecture And System

- **ARC-001** Otos UI implementation shall prefer SwiftUI, and shall use AppKit only when SwiftUI cannot provide the required capability or cannot meet verified responsiveness or performance requirements. [verified]
  Example: Given a UI surface is implemented, it uses SwiftUI unless the AppKit grid boundary (or another documented AppKit escape) is required for a verified capability or responsiveness need.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/ContentView.swift`, `OtosKit/OtosApp.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridBridge.swift`

- **ARC-002** Otos shall run unsandboxed and shall access library roots through normal filesystem paths, with App Sandbox, security-scoped bookmarks, bookmark schema, bookmark lifecycles, and sandbox-driven file access flows out of product scope. [verified]
  Example: Given the app is built, the project does not enable App Sandbox and library roots are ordinary filesystem paths.
  Implemented in: `Otos.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj`, `OtosKit/Models/CatalogPath.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`

- **ARC-002A** `script/smoke-run.sh` shall never terminate an in-flight import or reorganization. [verified]
  - The journals `active_file_operation` and `active_reorganization_component` shall be the durable busy truth (not `log show`), read via `sqlite3 -readonly` under WAL.
  - If reading the journals produces any sqlite error, then `script/smoke-run.sh` shall count it as busy.
  - Any journal row shall forbid `kill` except an import row in phase `abandoned`, which is cleanup owed to the next launch and nothing in flight (per-root output is diagnostic only).
  - `is_import_active` shall alias `has_active_operational_work`.
  Example: Given an import or reorganization journal row exists (even for a different root), script/smoke-run.sh --jank refuses to start and its cleanup trap leaves the app running instead of kill; given sqlite3 returns busy/corrupt, the script also treats the state as busy; given only an `abandoned` import row, or no journal row, the script proceeds and may quit_app.
  Implemented in: `script/lib/operational-busy.sh`, `script/smoke-run.sh`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStoreImportJournal.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStoreReorganizationJournal.swift`

- **ARC-003** `Store` shall use one actor as the single owner of GRDB, migrations, serialized initialization, root runtime state, per-root namespace-mutation epochs, scan and device-enumeration leases, removable-source attach/quiesce/resume/detach state, scan persistence cadence, all catalog queries, and the durable operational busy predicate (`hasActiveOperationalWork` over `active_file_operation` ∪ `active_reorganization_component`). [verified]
  - Pure SQL/mapping for scoped reads shall live in the `nonisolated` gateways `CatalogQueryGateway`/`EnrichmentClaimGateway`.
  - The actor shall keep only `timedRead`/`timedWrite`, `RootRegistry` snapshot capture, and `namespaceMutationEpoch`/`filesystemDirty`/`ScanLeaseController`/`reorganizationLeases` atomicity.
  Example: Given concurrent startup, mutation, or namespace-mutation-lease calls enter an unloaded Store, they await one initial load, so a lease for a persisted root is never denied as `rootNotRegistered` before roots load, and a catalog that cannot load denies it as `catalogUnavailable`; given catalog behavior is implemented, `Store` owns SQLite/scanning/runtime catalog truth, exposes cheap typed change notifications plus Store-owned query APIs for fixed libraries, removable sources, descriptor-ordered IDs, scoped filter options, visible asset details, and enrichment candidates; given a stale filesystem scan completes after an independent import, reorganization, or filesystem mutation, Store may upsert observed facts but suppresses missing media/companion deletion and retains typed dirty scopes for follow-up reconciliation; given a stale device enumeration completes after a newer owner is current, Store rejects the stale write/reconciliation; SwiftUI receives final projected catalog window state through `ViewModel`, and views do not call SQLite directly; given a Store-owned wrapper is called, it captures `roots`/`RootRegistry` outside `await db.read` and delegates pure SQL to the gateway so no scoped read can forget a scope dimension.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/RootRegistry.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogQueryGateway.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/EnrichmentClaimGateway.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModel.swift`

- **ARC-004** Otos shall use the normalized filesystem path for the current root location as library-root domain identity, with symlink components resolved for existing selected fixed roots before persistence. [verified]
  - Removable sources may additionally carry a stable source identity used to remap the current path.
  - SQLite rowid may be used only as a private foreign-key implementation detail.
  Example: Given a root is shown, selected, logged, or compared as product state, code uses `LibraryRootPath`; given a user or smoke script selects a symlinked directory such as a Desktop OneDrive shortcut, Store persists the resolved provider directory path so scanner enumeration and root-relative paths use the same spelling; given an older reachable saved root is symlink-spelled, Store repairs it to the resolved path during startup before applying runtime root state and migrates any existing thumbnail path/file to the normalized-root deterministic name; given the same removable source identity returns at a new mount or staging path, Store updates the root path for that source; given media rows reference a root in SQLite, they may use `library_root.id` only inside persistence internals.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Models/CatalogModels.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStorePersistence.swift`

- **ARC-005** CatalogWindowViewModel shall own the single window's final UI state, explicit sidebar-selection intent delivery, platform removable-source monitor lifetimes, startup device-staging sweep handoff, per-camera catalog tasks, and automatic sidebar selection through SidebarSelectionPolicy until the user explicitly chooses a source. [verified]
  Example: Given a sidebar row is chosen by the user, the ViewModel sets `userHasChosenSource` and sends the intent through one owned query-descriptor stream; given startup completes before any user sidebar click, the ViewModel keeps a restored browser place's scope if it still exists (APP-043) and otherwise applies `SidebarSelectionPolicy.defaultSelection` once (connected removable/camera by most-recent attach, else single fixed library, else All Media); given a removable volume or camera attaches after the first loaded state, the ViewModel jumps to that source through `SidebarSelectionPolicy.selectionOnAttach`, keyed on the Store's attachment generation so only a real attach moves the user; given a stale projected state proves the currently selected root was removed, the ViewModel applies `SidebarSelectionPolicy.selectionOnSelectedSourceRemoved` instead of leaving a dead selection; given removable volume or camera events arrive, the ViewModel routes them through the same lossless catalog mutation or Store-owned task path; given camera monitoring starts, stale device-staging directories are swept off the MainActor before ImageCapture monitoring begins; given the ViewModel ends, it cancels its state, monitor, camera, staging-sweep, and selection/mutation consumers before finishing their continuations.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/CatalogProjection.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/SidebarSelectionPolicy.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModel.swift`

- **ARC-006** Otos shall run catalog root sorting, fixed/removable source splitting, descriptor-ordered asset querying, sidebar-scope filtering, media kind/format filtering, title lookup, selected-root validation, availability hiding, device enumerating empty state, and empty-state selection outside the MainActor. [verified]
  Example: Given a store change, sidebar selection change, sort change, filter change, removable attach/quiesce/resume/detach, or camera enumeration change occurs, CatalogProjection asks Store for descriptor-ordered IDs and computes CatalogWindowState off the MainActor before CatalogWindowViewModel assigns UI state.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/CatalogProjection.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogWindowPolicy.swift`

- **ARC-007** Catalog source roots shall store the smallest current source metadata needed to distinguish fixed libraries, removable volumes, and MTP cameras: kind, optional deviceIdentity, optional raw display name, and optional lastSeenAt. [verified]
  - A source's identity shall be a `DeviceIdentity`, the one owner of its persisted text and its transience.
  - A volume's identity shall be its UUID, or its mount path when it reports none.
  - A camera's identity shall be its persistent id, or its name when it reports none.
  - An identity from a mount path or a name, or one disambiguated by mount path, shall be transient.
  Example: Given Store loads roots, `LibraryRoot` carries source kind and display identity separately from transient availability/activity; given a removable source is attached, Store records or updates `device_identity`, `display_name`, and `last_seen_at`; given two mounted removable volumes report the same UUID, runtime source identity is disambiguated with the mount path without adding a durable fingerprint or source table; given Projection builds state, fixed libraries and removable sources are split by `kind`; given schema is inspected, no separate source table, durable scan ID, content fingerprint, identity sidecar, or app-owned original copy is introduced for removable media.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Models/CatalogModels.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStorePersistence.swift`

- **ARC-008** Otos shall type catalog domain identifiers at app boundaries.
  Example: Given code models a library root path, media relative path, or media asset identity, it uses LibraryRootPath, MediaRelativePath, or MediaAssetID instead of bare strings or concatenated string identifiers across app boundaries.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Models/CatalogModels.swift`
  unverified: typed identifiers at every app boundary need behavioral boundary coverage; current proof is limited to the implementation boundary

- **ARC-009** Otos shall treat filesystem scans as idempotent reconciliation operations, not durable domain entities, and shall not store durable scan IDs, scan-history tables, resumable scan records, first-seen markers, last-seen markers, missing-since markers, or tombstone state. [verified]
  Example: Given a scan completes, currently observed rows are inserted or updated and missing rows are deleted for that root; given a scan is cancelled or fails, previously cataloged rows are preserved; given Store guards callbacks with a process-local path, private root-row, and in-memory lease generation, that concurrency bookkeeping is neither persisted nor exposed as durable scan identity; given schema or docs are inspected, no durable scan identity or scan history is represented as product state.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`

- **ARC-010** Catalog root rows shall combine durable root/source identity with separate transient unknown, available, or unavailable availability and scan/enumeration activity, while LibraryRoot shall remain durable identity and source metadata only. [verified]
  Example: Given a database-loaded fixed root's folder is there, its row reports `available` and its rows show before any scan starts, and given its folder is gone, `unavailable` (one `stat` at load, ADR 0008); given a newly added root has not yet been checked, its row reports `unknown`; given a removable source detaches, its row reports `unavailable` without deleting rows; given a camera is enumerating, its row reports enumeration activity separately from availability; given a view renders roots, it receives `[CatalogLibraryState]` without joining separate root, availability, activity, or ejectability collections.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Models/CatalogModels.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/SidebarView.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/SettingsView.swift`

- **ARC-011** Settings library and removable-source management shall consume projected root rows and catalog intents only. [verified]
  - The Import tab shall receive only ImportPreferences bindings/closures.
  - Settings may render availability/last-seen facts but shall not consume scan status, media assets, browser state, raw catalog state, plan payloads, or the whole catalog window ViewModel.
  Example: Given Settings renders the Libraries tab, `SettingsView` receives fixed `[CatalogLibraryState]` rows plus add/remove closures; given it renders Removable Media, it receives removable source rows plus a Forget closure; given it renders Import, it receives preferences plus a save closure; it displays name/path/availability or last-seen facts and does not display scan activity or receive `ViewModel`, media assets, or sidebar state.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Views/SettingsView.swift`, `OtosKit/OtosApp.swift`

- **ARC-013** Main-window catalog state shall distinguish sidebar scope, query descriptor, grid identity structure, visible tile detail invalidation, and visible tile detail reads with precise names.
  Example: Given code stores the selected sidebar row, it uses `sidebarSelection`; given code publishes the media browser state, `mediaGrid.descriptor` identifies the resolved sidebar scope, media filter, and sort order, `orderedAssetIDs` defines display order, and `detailRefreshGeneration` invalidates visible detail reads without carrying per-asset values; given cells need current values, the AppKit grid faults visible details by ID through the Store-backed `MediaTileDetailReader` (ARC-059) instead of receiving a full asset map.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Models/CatalogModels.swift`
  unverified: state naming and visible-ID detail reads need behavioral coverage; current proof is limited to the implementation boundary

- **ARC-014** Otos shall have exactly one main window as a permanent product and architecture invariant, and multiple main windows shall not be designed, documented, proposed, deferred, or kept open as a future architecture path. [verified]
  Example: Given the app scene is inspected, the main catalog window uses singleton SwiftUI Window("Otos", id: "main"), the main scene does not use WindowGroup, and the standard New Window command is replaced or unavailable for the main window.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/OtosApp.swift`

- **ARC-015** SwiftUI views shall not orchestrate catalog snapshot streams or projection invalidation. [verified]
  Example: Given ContentView is inspected, it renders CatalogWindowViewModel state and binds controls, but it does not subscribe to catalog notifications, call CatalogProjection, call CatalogStore, coordinate .onChange catalog work, or define view-owned update(snapshot:) catalog behavior.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/ContentView.swift`

- **ARC-016** CatalogWindowViewModel shall be a MainActor adapter and explicit intent/task owner only, not the catalog truth owner or projection scheduler. [verified]
  Example: Given code is inspected, the ViewModel owns named startup, state-consumption, query-forwarding, and mutation-forwarding tasks, final SwiftUI state assignment, synchronous sidebar/sort/filter intent submission, scalar stall correlation, and local query-control state that is not rolled back by stale projected states; public add/ensure/remove intents create no task or direct Store call; SQLite, filesystem traversal, scanning, dirty cadence, sorting, filtering, availability hiding, and selection resolution remain in `Store` or `Projection`.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModel.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogProjection.swift`

- **ARC-017** The regular-file catalog scanner shall skip macOS package descendants by default, record skipped package diagnostics, and retain a per-root skippedPackageCount from the last completed scan for later import-footer attention. Package-container media support requires an explicit future media-container decision. [verified]
  Example: Given a library contains Photos Library.photoslibrary with a file inside plus an ordinary file beside it, the scan completes, catalogs the ordinary file, does not catalog the package descendant, records catalog.scan.skippedPackage with the package path and available content type identifier, and Store reports a positive skipped-package count for that root.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/CatalogScanner.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/RootRuntimeState.swift`

- **ARC-018** `Store` shall expose cheap typed catalog change notifications instead of a UI snapshot publication stream, and all catalog reads shall go through Store-owned query APIs (ADR 0005). [verified]
  - When a write changes existing facts, the Store shall emit `.changed(CatalogChangedFacts, rows:)`, naming the assets whose rows it changed or removed when the write knows them (`CatalogChangedRows`).
  - An enrichment batch, a metadata re-read, an edit, provisional thumbnails, a scan or listing batch and a scan's or resync's deletions shall name their rows, and a deletion shall name the Live Photo partners it leaves (`AssetRowDeletion`).
  - A root's own state — its order, a scan or listing starting or ending — shall name `none`, and `named` shall always name at least one row (`CatalogNamedRows`).
  - A root's availability moving, which changes which rows are in reach, shall be announced by the one function that sets it, unnamed; any other write shall be unnamed.
  - The change shall also carry the kinds of fact the write touched — rows shown, capture dates, kinds and formats, grouping identifiers, choices and stack keys, locations, place names, faces, copies (digests, render signatures, pairs kept together), On This Day's search labels, what search read of a row (`searchContent`: an embedding or text reading written, which a description waits for), stored images and the edit they show (`images`), the bytes' record (`mediaRecord`), what can change a stack (`stacks`: a date, kind or grouping of a still in a burst or sharing its folder with a still it could pair with), detail — as the Store knows them at the write.
  - An enrichment batch shall compare what SQLite stored before and after each row.
  - Every other write shall name the kinds it wrote and never more — a row's presence (`presence`: a Trash mark or move, a put-back, a source's hide, a deletion, an attachment's awaiting rows), its file's facts (`fileFacts`), its path (`movedRow`: every kind but place names), an edit, a settled import (`settledImport`).
  - A change that follows no write shall name the runtime state it reports (a root's reach `membership` unnamed: the catalog's load, an attachment; a root's own state `membership` with `none`: a root added with no rows yet, a removal that failed, a detach, a scan's skipped packages, the Store's shutdown).
  - If an operation wrote nothing, then it shall send nothing, and only a removed library's `.libraryRemoved` shall stand for every kind.
  - Projection shall advance `detailRefreshGeneration` for every change.
  - Projection shall repeat the ordered-asset read, the filter-options read, the stack candidates' read (`stackInputs`: rows shown, choices, `stacks`), the stack collapse and a shown search's match (keyed also by `PhotoSearchIndex.revision`, which moves with every change to the index's memory; its fact matches read rows shown, capture dates, locations and faces, `searchFactInputs`, only for a query naming facts) only when a kind of fact each depends on was touched since its last answer, which it keeps with the revisions its pass started from (`CatalogProjectionReads`).
  - The options shall be read after the window publishes (`docs/research/2026-11-14-scope-switch-and-background-laps.md`).
  - Duplicates and People shall each be asked for a build only after a publication that took up a change of a kind their answer is built from (`DuplicatesProjection.inputs`: rows shown, grouping, copies, kinds, capture dates, the record, choices, images; `PeopleAssignment.inputs`: faces, rows shown, grouping, kinds, capture dates, choices, images), so a faces write never rebuilds the groups and a digest or render signature never re-runs People (`docs/research/2026-11-14-derived-owners-and-face-checks.md`).
  - When a scan, device-listing or source-listing batch only inserted rows (`CatalogRowChanges`, `ScannedFileUpsert.inserted`), the Store shall emit `.assetsAdded`, and, for a new row a source's digest makes a copy of one the catalog holds, also `.changed([.detail, .copies])` naming it, which Duplicates and People take up.
  - Because such rows carry nothing derived yet, Projection shall publish them without asking Duplicates or People for a build; it shall ask People only for its unchecked tally (`PeopleAssignment.requestTally`, one count read republished with the last state), since each new still is one not yet checked for faces.
  - `PhotoSearchIndex`, `PlaceNameReader`, `PreviewFaceReader` and `RenderSignatureRefresher` shall not read for an insert-only batch.
  - A batch that changes a held row's file facts shall emit `.changed(.fileFacts)` and one that hides or deletes it `.changed(.presence)`, naming the rows (`docs/research/2026-11-12-listing-batch-cost.md`).
  - When a listing batch changes only a held row's source facts — its item ID, its server path's digest, whether the source holds images of it — the Store shall emit `.changed([.membership, .copies, .images, .detail])` naming it (`CatalogRowChanges.recordChanged`).
  - When a source answers that it holds no images of rows, the Store shall emit `.changed([.images, .detail])` naming the rows it marked, since an empty search counts them as getting no thumbnail.
  - A video's reading that takes or replaces its stored face frames shall name `.images`.
  - Each owner of a derived product — render signatures, preview faces, place names, the search index, descriptions — shall wake only on a change of a kind its own candidate SQL reads, derived from its statements' regions (`CatalogOwnerInputs`, over `CatalogChangedFacts.writes`, the one table of the columns each kind stands for), less the kinds its own writes name and with which roots are reachable.
  - Rows only added shall wake none of the derived-product owners, and a change of the user's locale shall wake the place name reader.
  Example: Given Store content changes, subscribers receive a lightweight invalidation event; given a library is successfully removed, the lossless event also carries its typed root path so Projection can reset a selected root even if same-path re-add coalesces before publication; given UI state is needed, `Projection` asks Store for current root rows and ordered asset IDs, then increments a scalar detail-refresh generation so detail-only writes are not equality-suppressed; given an enrichment batch changes no capture date, kind, format, grouping identifier or location, Store names its detail (and the record, faces and copies it read) and Projection refreshes visible detail without reading the order or the options; given a new person (a faces write), People is rebuilt and the duplicate groups are not, and given copies kept together, the groups are rebuilt and People is not; given a batch moves a capture date, Projection reads the order again and not the options; given copies kept together under a library scope, neither is read again; given a choice (a stack fact), the order is read again and the options are not; given faces read under a library scope, the order is not read, and given a face given the person shown, that person's order is; given a search shown and a lap that took up only a choice, the index is not asked to match again, and given the index's memory moved, it is; given visible cells need details, the grid asks Store for those IDs only; given a listing batch of new rows, Store emits `.assetsAdded`, the window shows them and the duplicate groups are not rebuilt, while a batch that changes, hides or deletes a held row emits `.changed(.fileFacts)` or `.changed(.presence)` naming it and they are; given each Store operation that once sent `.all`, it sends the kinds it wrote, or its runtime state, or nothing; given a new listed row whose digest another copy holds, Store emits `.assetsAdded` and a copies change naming it; given a deleted Live Photo still, the change names its movie; given a root lost by a scan that failed, it is announced unnamed, apart from the scan's own `none`, which wakes no owner of a derived product; given a source with no preview of a row, the row's images are announced; given a video's new reading finds no face, the change names its images. Given a place-name write, a duplicates fact and a library added, each owner rings for exactly the changes that touch its inputs and never for its own writes; given a locale change, the place name reader wakes; every column an owner's SQL reads is named by a kind of fact.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogProjection.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogProjectionBuildTypes.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogProjectionWindowStateBuild.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStorePersistence.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogSQLTypes.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/PhotoSearchIndex.swift`

- **ARC-019** CatalogProjection shall own one publication loop for CatalogWindowState, driven by a doorbell and two revision counters (ADR 0005). [verified]
  - Every intent and Store change shall add to `requested`, and a pass shall advance `covered` to the `requested` it started from only when its build succeeds, so a request made while a pass runs is never lost.
  - Direct sidebar, sort, and filter intents shall ring the doorbell immediately, while Store-originated background changes shall coalesce on a roughly one-second cadence, with at most one projection publication running at a time.
  - While the published window shows no item, a change of which rows exist (`.membership`) shall publish at once: such a window has no grid a reorder could cost, and what it waits for is its first rows — a newly added library's, which its walk hands to the Store as a first batch of `CatalogScanner.firstBatchCount` (256) files and then a batch a cadence, so they are written in tens of milliseconds rather than after a cadence's worth of files.
  - An intent whose descriptor the build in flight does not answer shall cancel that build, and an intent for another sidebar row shall cancel an owed filter-options read.
  - Superseded work shall publish nothing even if it finishes, cover nothing and ring the loop for the new request at once: the pass never waits for the cancelled work to end, which may finish its cancelled read while the next pass runs.
  - The first pass of an intent shall answer every read Projection holds an answer for from that answer — the order and collapse of All Media, held while the user is elsewhere, and of the last other scope; the import batches; the place-name languages — even when a fact it depends on moved since.
  - When a fact had moved since, the first pass shall publish that window and cover nothing, and the next pass shall read again what moved and republish, so a return to a scope already read paints without a read.
  - A store change shall never cancel the build in flight; teardown shall cancel it and it publishes nothing.
  - The filter's options shall never hold the window back, nor the pass after it.
  - A pass that owes the options shall publish the window with the last options read for the same row, or none, ask for them and cover its request.
  - The options shall be read as their own work, one read at a time with their own revision counter, kept and republished when the read ends, and cancelled by an intent for another row.
  - The options shall be read with every copy of identical files and shall never depend on which file is a copy.
  - The People gallery shall read no order, stacks, search or options.
  - If a Store read fails, then Projection shall publish nothing: the last published state stands, the request stays outstanding, and the next intent or cadence tick retries it.
  Example: Given Store changes arrive rapidly, Projection coalesces them on the cadence, does not start a second publication while one is in flight, and reruns after the in-flight publication when a request arrived during it, including a Store change; given a user changes sidebar scope, sort, or filter, Projection rings the doorbell for an immediate publication; given a one-hour cadence and an empty window, a library added is listed and its first rows are shown, while once the window shows items another library added waits for the tick; given a walk of 300 files on a one-hour cadence, the Store receives 256 files and then 44; given a build's read throws, nothing is published from that call and the next intent publishes; given the last subscriber ends while a build runs, nothing is published; given an intent for another scope arrives while a build is parked, that build is cancelled and never published and the new scope publishes; given a store change arrives while a build is parked, the build publishes; given the superseded build ignores its cancellation, the new scope publishes while it is still parked and what it returns is never published; given a return to All Media from a library with no fact moved, the order is not read and every state published shows the held order; given a capture date moved while the user was in the library, the return first publishes All Media's held order, then reads the order once and publishes the moved one; given Projection starts, it registers the Store change stream before relying on the loop so detail-only Store writes after startup advance `detailRefreshGeneration`.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/CatalogProjection.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogProjectionWindowStateBuild.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogScanner.swift`

- **ARC-020** Otos shall hold library availability as transient in-memory state with explicit unknown, available, and unavailable values, and shall never persist it to SQLite. [verified]
  Example: Given roots load from SQLite without scanning, they begin unknown; given a new validated root is added, it begins available; given the pre-scan reachability check runs, unknown transitions to available or unavailable; given a root is unavailable, its catalog rows are preserved and no reconciliation deletion occurs.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`

- **ARC-021** Catalog UI shall hide unknown and unavailable libraries' media while preserving their cataloged rows; grid and Settings surfaces may describe availability, but the sidebar shall render library names without transient status subtitles. [verified]
  Example: Given an unknown library is selected, the grid shows `Checking Library`; given All Media has no visible rows and at least one unknown root, it shows `Checking Libraries`; given Sidebar renders libraries, rows show the folder icon and library display name only, with no `Checking`, `Scanning`, or `Unavailable` subtitle; Settings may still label unknown roots `Checking`; unavailable grid states continue to show `Library Unavailable` or `No Available Media` as applicable.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/CatalogProjection.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridView.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/SidebarView.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/SettingsView.swift`

- **ARC-022** Store-owned SQLite queries shall produce catalog display order and filter membership from one `CatalogQueryDescriptor`, and `Projection` shall publish descriptor-scoped ordered IDs without sorting full asset values or carrying a full value map. [verified]
  - Projection shall ask Store for ordered IDs when a change can have altered them (ARC-018), and may ask Store for separate counts only to distinguish empty-state meanings when no assets are visible.
  - Every scoped read — ordered IDs, cataloged count, visible count, filter options — shall resolve its `SidebarSelection` through the one `CatalogScopePredicate` produced by `RootRegistry`, so no read can honor one scope dimension and omit another.
  - The predicate shall carry root membership, the import-batch stamp and the location constraint (ADR 0040), and a scope that requires a location shall join `asset_metadata` so the ordered-ID read also carries each row's coordinate.
  - Every order — date (v55), name and size (v69) — the stack candidates' folder walk the filter's types share, and the import batches' read shall walk indexes that carry every column their row tests read (whether a row is shown, and whether it is a Live Photo's movie), so a row is tested without its table row being read.
  - The order indexes shall also carry each row's format and choice (v76), so a file-type filter and a choices filter that takes Not Rated rows test each walked row from its index entry, a Places filter finds each walked row's cell from its coordinate in `idx_asset_metadata_location`, and the order read carries a row's capture time only under a date order, the one order whose index holds it.
  - Every order of a wide scope, under every filter but a chosen rows' seek, shall walk its index in order, sort nothing and read no `media_asset` or `asset_metadata` table row, and return the order a plain reading of the table gives (`docs/research/2026-11-17-every-order-the-window-asks-for.md`).
  - After changes that moved only capture dates of rows they name, a date order of a scope by roots alone shall re-read those rows alone by key and merge them into the order it holds, which is then the order a full read gives (`CatalogOrderPatch`, ADR 0005).
  - The order held for a scope shall answer every search over it: a search narrows the order after it is read, so typing, changing or clearing a search reads no order, and a scope's unsearched collapse is held beside its last search's.
  - A scope that names its rows by a key — an import batch, a set of identical copies, a stack's members, one person's photos (found from the person's faces through `idx_face_person_photo`, each then by its key) — shall read them by that key's index and sort the few it finds, for its order, its filter's types, its counts and its place options alike (a person's order reads the person's own rows of `person_photo` instead, below), never by walking the date order, every row or every named place cell.
  - Such a keyed scope's stacks shall be read from its own folders while its order holds at most 2,000 rows, and shall be every stack of its roots past that (`PhotoStackSQL.rowsSpanMostRows`, ARC-062).
  - A wide scope filtered to Liked or Disliked rows shall read them by the chosen rows' index (`idx_media_asset_chosen`, v71), which holds only them, and sort the few it finds, for its order and its count; a filter that takes Not Rated rows shall walk the order.
  - A person's order shall read the person's own range of `person_photo` (v78, ADR 0047), which holds every column the order and the window's tests read, and sort it, so its pages are in proportion to the person's photos at every size.
  - The photos the person is in only through a copy shall be found from the person's photos that have copies, in the same compound read, and the order shall be the one a walk of the person's faces gave.
  - A person's other reads, On This Day's day and a map area shall seek their rows only while the seek's candidates — the person's faces, the day's index entries, the located rows of the area's cells, counted from their indexes in the same read — times a measured ratio (40, 2 and 10, `SeekSize`) are fewer than the rows a walk of the order visits.
  - Past that ratio, those reads shall walk the order and test each row in it, and their stacks shall be every stack of their roots: the most photographed person, a day holding most of a library and a world-sized area read the walk's pages, not a page or more per row (`docs/research/2026-11-15-large-narrow-scopes.md`).
  - When every change since they were read moved only locations or file types of rows it names, or the cells' names, the filter's options of a row whose scope is a whole set of roots shall be re-read by key: each named row's type and cell replace what it gave in the held tally (`CatalogFilterOptionTally`), patched in place, with the names of the cells those rows give, and of every cell the tally counts when the names moved, each by its key.
  - Any other change shall read the filter's options whole, in one walk of the scope's rows through `idx_media_asset_stack_candidate`, then the names of the cells that walk counted, each by its key, never every named cell's.
  Example: Given a publication, Projection asks Store for ordered IDs for the resolved descriptor and available roots; given all selected assets are hidden only because roots are unavailable, Projection asks Store for the selected scope's cataloged count and publishes `.noAvailableMedia` rather than `.noMedia`; given an active filter hides every visible item while unfiltered assets exist, Projection publishes `.noFilterMatches`, including when the scope is an import batch whose every row that filter excludes; given a scope selects no root at all, every read answers empty without querying SQLite; given a window state, `orderedAssetIDs` is the descriptor-scoped ordered visible structure and visible details are fetched separately by ID. Given location writes to named rows of an owner-shaped catalog, each is read by key, no whole read follows, and the options shown after each equal a whole read; given a renamed cell, only the names are read again.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogSQLTypes.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/RootRegistry.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogQueryGateway.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogProjection.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogWindowPolicy.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/CatalogFilterOptionTally.swift`

- **ARC-023** The media collection shall permit at most one structural apply in flight and retain only the newest pending state (DES-074, DES-119). [verified]
  - Within that one in-flight slot a structural apply shall build its structure (`MediaGridStructure`: ordered identities, day sections as contiguous ranges, identity index — one linear pass, never AppKit's diffable snapshot, whose build grows with day sections × items and took 13–15 s at 158,000 items over 5,000 days), its diff summary and, for an animated apply, its batch of inserts, deletes and moves off the main actor and only then hand it to the collection view on the main actor.
  - The exception shall be a grid whose order is made from the one on screen by edits naming fewer than 1,000 positions (`CatalogGridContent.edit(from:)`), whose build costs the sections and the edit and runs and commits in the main-actor turn that delivered it, so a grid arriving while the structure builds parks and is applied after it.
  - An apply shall be in flight from its admission until its commit returns and never for the motion that commit starts, its completion running as the commit returns, so a grid arriving while earlier applies' tiles still move is committed at once, each moving tile changing course from where it is drawn, and no apply waits for another's motion (DES-074, DES-119).
  - The collection view shall hold the order as one AppKit section (an item's index path its position, a day header's keyed by its day so it keeps its path while other days come and go), the day sections being the layout's, so no update costs AppKit work per day.
  - Every derived batch shall turn the structure on screen into the next one, sections matched by day, a day only in the old structure losing its header and one only in the new gaining one, an item that changed day moved wherever it went, within each surviving day the longest in-order run of its own items left in place and every other item moved.
  - The summary and the batch shall read one comparison (`MediaGridStructureChange`) that pairs days by key, compares each pair's items from both ends, and looks up by identity only what lies between the runs a day shares and the items of unpaired days, so a reorder's build follows the change rather than the library.
  - Projection shall publish every grid order of the same query as an edit of the last publication's (`CatalogGridContent.Edit`: removing items, `removing`, removing and inserting them, `edited`, or the walk of a read's order beside the last one, `Edit.between`, an item removed and inserted being a move), each content keeping the edit from every earlier order still held with the edits between composed (`Edit.followed(by:)`), so the grid compares two orders only for a new query's, published whole.
  - A grid whose order was made from the one on screen by edits, through any publications the window skipped, shall be taken in by the edit's positions, comparing no item and looking up only what the edit inserted, its sections what a fresh build makes and its batch the edit's deletes, inserts and moves with the headers of the days it emptied or started.
  - The layout shall take the structure's sections and header keys in one value rather than asking per section.
  - Detail-only updates for unchanged IDs shall requery and reconfigure existing visible items in place without AppKit reload/reuse.
  - Visible IDs shall be pinned once per reconfigure/merge/dequeue pass before store mutations.
  - Scroll-driven viewport reporting, visible detail queries, preheat look-ahead and hover recomputation shall coalesce on a trailing edge bounded by a maximum wait (`MediaGridScrollWorkCadence`), and shall skip a new detail query when the visible identity list is unchanged.
  - A trailing edge alone is cancelled and rescheduled by each bounds notification and so never fires while scrolling continues — measured on a live session at 3,594 bounds ticks to 221 flushes, 6.1%, with a busiest second of 153 ticks and one flush; the maximum wait is what serves a sustained fling.
  - Structural applies shall not prune sticky tile-detail knowledge before the post-apply visible detail request.
  - "What is visible" shall be answered from the collection view's attached items when it has any and from the layout otherwise, so an apply into a grid at rest still requests detail for the rows on screen instead of waiting for a scroll tick that a still cursor never produces.
  - Hover shall have exactly one owner that assigns the curation control to at most one tile and recomputes from the pointer's real position whenever scroll-driven work runs and after every structural apply, because AppKit delivers `mouseEntered` to a tile scrolling under a stationary cursor but no `mouseExited` to the one scrolling away.
  - That hover owner shall re-assert hover on every tile render rather than only when the hovered asset changes, because a tile clears its own control whenever its content resets and both the asset-identity and index-path change guards suppress the repair while the pointer holds on one thumbnail.
  - Scroll-driven hover recomputation shall report the current index path unconditionally and dedup on asset identity, because after a reorder an unchanged index path may hold a different asset.
  - Same-scope same-membership reorders (enrichment trickle) shall animate while scrolling, and animated applies shall keep animated but set without animation so the true-diff still runs without a visible move animation.
  - While the user scrolls (a tick inside AppKit's live-scroll bracket or while a scroll-wheel event is handled; a bounds change Otos makes itself, such as an apply keeping its anchor or a focus scroll, holds nothing), the mutation gate shall hold same-scope structural-apply adoption (newest-wins pending) until quiet, while scope-changed grids bypass the hold and zoom swaps remain unaffected.
  - Reorder-only applies shall skip pruning (membership is unchanged).
  - Apply-policy summarize shall read membership from that comparison, the previous structure's identity index answering only the looked-up items, so no apply rebuilds `Set(previous)`.
  - Scope-changed reorders and large membership changes (`large membership`: 1,000 or more items inserted plus removed) shall keep AppKit's reloadData-class fallback (not animated); smaller insert/remove changes animate at any grid size.
  - The measured same-scope trickle synchronous apply call (the structural-apply line's `commitMs`) at ~23k is in the low tens of milliseconds for K = 1–3 movers.
  - Repeated preheat requests whose IDs are already covered by the in-flight preheat task shall reuse that task instead of cancelling it, and partial AppKit prefetch cancellation shall remove only cancelled IDs rather than dropping the whole burst.
  Example: Given grid identity or detail-refresh generation changes while an apply is active, no second apply starts concurrently, intermediate pending states are replaced, and the newest state is processed after completion; given five hides and a Put Back each delivered while every earlier apply's tiles still move, each is on screen as its commit returns, with every earlier motion still running; given IDs and day buckets are unchanged, visible details are re-read and visible items are reconfigured in place, and reload is not called for that refresh path; given a visible reconfigure pass, the controller pins the current visible identity set once before updating tiles; given an act published on top of a read that moved photos which the grid never showed, at 160,000 items, the grid takes it in by the composed edit, comparing none of them; given scroll bounds change rapidly with the same visible identities, the controller coalesces scroll-driven detail/preheat work and does not restart an identical visible detail query on every tick; given same-scope grid states arrive during live scroll, the gate parks newest-wins until quiet and then applies once; given the grid scrolls by Otos's own hand, no hold begins and the next grid is applied at once, while a scope-changed grid applies immediately and a zoom request still starts when idle; given a structural apply runs, it does not erase known tile details before the post-apply visible detail request merges fresh rows; given a scope-changed reorder or large membership apply recycles visible AppKit items, APP-015 keeps previously painted same-asset thumbnails from blanking through synchronous decoded repaint and downgrade guards; given scroll look-ahead asks for IDs already covered by an in-flight preheat task, the controller does not restart the same Store detail read; given AppKit cancels only a subset of the current preheat IDs, the in-flight burst continues for the remaining IDs and cancelled IDs do not merge or warm when the read finishes.; given a structural apply inserts rows while the pointer is still and no scroll follows, the post-apply pass requests visible detail for those rows rather than reporting none visible; given the pointer rests over a tile and the strip is scrolled by less than one tile, the control clears on that still-attached tile rather than staying lit on every tile the pointer passed; given the pointer holds on one thumbnail while that tile re-renders mid-scroll, the control is re-asserted in the same pass and does not flicker off until an unrelated apply repairs it
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridBridge.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridStructuralApply.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridStructure.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridLayout.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/CatalogModels.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridPrefetch.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridMutationGate.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridSupport.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridAnimation.swift`

- **ARC-024** The Store shall make concurrent callers share one Store-owned initial catalog load, and shall apply loaded state exactly once before mutations proceed. [verified]
  Example: Given startup is suspended in a database read and a catalog mutation intent arrives, both calls await the same initialization result and the later mutation remains present after startup completes.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`

- **ARC-025** Otos shall give every fixed library restored by the initial database load exactly one automatic launch reconciliation, even when a mutation initialized the Store before ordinary app startup requested it (ADR 0002, "Change Journal"). [verified]
  - When the journal can vouch for the root, its volume's FSEvents history since the position the catalog stored for it shall be replayed and reconciled like live events, with no walk.
  - The root shall be walked in full (`op=catalog.scan reason=`) when it cannot be read (`unreadable`), its volume keeps no history or the position is ahead of it (`noHistory`), no position is stored (`firstWalk`), the volume's event database is another (`uuidChanged`), the root folder is another (`rootChanged`), or a replayed event says the journal lost track (`dropped`, `rootChanged`, root-wide `events`).
  - A position shall be stored only once every event before it is applied and no walk is owed (ADR 0002, "Change Journal").
  - An event the stream delivers at or before the ID it was started from (the stored position, or the probe a walking root's walk started after; `FullHistory` delivers the whole history chunk holding that ID) shall be treated as already reflected in the catalog and shall read nothing, so a newly added library's first walk leaves it current.
  Example: Given a saved root exists and a catalog mutation enters the unloaded Store first, the later start() still reconciles that restored root once; a root newly added after loading receives only its add-triggered walk; given a replay with no events, no directory is read, no file statted and no write transaction runs; given a replay naming two folders, exactly those two are read; given a file deleted while Otos was not running, its one replayed event purges its row; given `MustScanSubDirs` at the root, `UserDropped`, `KernelDropped`, `EventIdsWrapped`, `RootChanged`, `Mount` or `Unmount`, the root is walked; given another event database, another root folder, a position ahead of the stream or no history, the root is walked from a stream started before the walk; given an event delivered during a walk, it is applied after the walk and no position passes the walk it overtook; given a newly added library whose history older than the walk's probe holds its own creation, nothing is read after its first walk and the walk's position is stored; given an event after the probe during a first walk, exactly that path is read again; given replayed events at or before the stored position, nothing is read.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/RootChangeJournal.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogFilesystemWatcher.swift`

- **ARC-026** The Store shall accept scan and device-enumeration callbacks only while the same normalized root path, private root-row persistence instance, and in-memory lease generation remain current. [verified]
  Example: Given an active root is removed and the same path is added again before its cancelled scanner returns, every old batch/completion/cancellation/failure callback is ignored and cannot change replacement rows, availability, activity, or queue progress; given a second MTP enumeration starts for the same root before the first completes, the first enumeration can no longer write batches or reconcile missing rows.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`

- **ARC-027** Projection shall retain the resolved fallback for a removed selected library unless a newer explicit sidebar intent chooses another scope; when All Media is hidden because only one fixed library exists, invalid .allMedia shall resolve to that library through SidebarSelectionPolicy. [verified]
  Example: Given a selected root is removed and the same path is re-added before the next publication, Store's lossless typed removal event resets Projection to All Media and the replacement remains unselected unless only one fixed library exists, in which case the resolved scope is that library; given a newer explicit intent arrived while an older build awaited, that newer input is not overwritten.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogProjection.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogWindowPolicy.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/SidebarSelectionPolicy.swift`

- **ARC-028** CatalogWindowViewModel state consumption shall not strongly retain the ViewModel while awaiting projected state. [verified]
  Example: Given all external ViewModel ownership is released while its Projection stream is active, the ViewModel deinitializes, cancels state consumption, terminates the Projection subscription, and receives no later state.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModel.swift`

- **ARC-029** Otos shall deliver catalog add, ensure, and remove intents through one ViewModel-owned, lossless, serial mutation stream. [verified]
  Example: Given catalog mutation commands are submitted rapidly, Store begins them in submission order with at most one ViewModel-originated mutation active; sidebar selection remains independently responsive; given the ViewModel ends, no queued command begins afterward, while an already-dequeued Store transaction may finish safely.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModel.swift`

- **ARC-030** Otos shall treat the filesystem as the single source of truth, with the catalog and every overlay a subordinate projection that defers to the filesystem when they disagree. Derived overlay (catalog rows, thumbnails, enrichment) is rebuildable; import-batch stamps are non-rebuildable overlay that require additive schema migration. [verified]
  Example: Given the catalog and a reachable root disagree about a file, a completed scan reconciles the catalog to the filesystem; given docs describe rebuildable projection, they also name import history as the non-rebuildable exception that forbids destructive schema reset.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogScanner.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStorePersistence.swift`

- **ARC-031** Otos shall never take media hostage: no opaque app-owned vault, nothing Otos derives or authors written into the user's media folders, and no generated sidecars for identity or authored overlay. [verified]
  - Existing user-owned useful companions (`.xmp` / `.srt`) may be inventoried and transferred only during explicit import/reorganization.
  - Short-lived, journaled, hidden `.otos-import-staging` and `.otos-reorg-*` paths (and operation-created directories) may exist inside a user library only for an in-flight import or reorganization; they shall be removed by commit, rollback, or recovery and are never an app-owned vault.
  - The catalog itself may be exported if portability is needed.
  Example: Given Otos stores overlay, it writes only inside Application Support and never into a user media folder except the explicit journaled staging/temp carve-out above; source and docs contain no sidecar *writer*; companions are inventoried in media_companion, never as media_asset.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStorePersistence.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/MediaCompanionModels.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/ReorganizationExecutor.swift`

- **ARC-032** Otos shall change the user's files only through explicit, opt-in, user-initiated actions; by default nothing on disk changes. [verified]
  Example: Given no explicit user action, no original is moved, renamed, or deleted; current add/remove-library and scan paths read media and write only the app catalog.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`

- **ARC-033** All overlay for an item shall share one lifecycle: held while the item exists inside a tracked library root, purged in full only on proven deletion, removable-source retention, or explicit library-root removal, and never on temporary absence. [verified]
  - Under removable-source retention, a card or camera not attached and unseen for 90 days shall be forgotten at launch through the same removal as Forget; detach stamps when it was last seen; a root a source is attaching to is left, and a source that attaches at a forgotten root's mount point while its removal runs waits for it and attaches as a new source.
  - Under explicit library-root removal, a camera that re-attaches while its removal deletes its staging folder shall wait for that removal and only then make the folder it attaches at.
  - Asset identity shall be the filesystem path, with no durable identifiers, content fingerprints, generated UUIDs, or generated sidecars.
  - When the move witness (ADR 0034) proves a move outside Otos, the move shall keep its overlay: a newly seen path whose file identifier, size and creation time match exactly one row on the same volume whose own path is gone takes that row, children included.
  - A photo's edit recipe shall be overlay on its row (ADR 0036): it moves with the row, import overlay reuse carries it to the library copy, Move to Trash's undo puts it back, and it is purged with the row.
  Example: Given an item exists under a tracked root, its overlay is retained; given a completed scan of a reachable root no longer observes it, its catalog row and any derivatives are purged; given an armed available root receives a regular-file remove FSEvent and that exact path is absent when Store checks it, the single catalog row and derivatives for that path are purged; given the user removes a library root from Otos, rows and derived overlay for that root are purged without changing originals; given the original's root is temporarily unreachable (ejected/offline volume, unreachable root) or a dataless placeholder that still enumerates, nothing is purged; given a camera attached on day 0 and detached on day 5, a launch on day 94 keeps it and a launch on day 96 forgets it with its rows; given a card forgotten so at launch and a different card mounting at its mount point, in either order of the removal and the attachment, the Store's roots and the catalog's `library_root` rows agree and the new card is kept; given a camera re-attaching while its removal is deleting its staging folder, the re-attached camera is a root and its staging folder exists; given a camera returning without some files it had, those files' rows are purged once its catalog completes; given a user-excluded selective-sync subtree inside a reachable root, reconciliation currently cannot distinguish it from a deletion and purges it, a documented forward edge case in ADR 0008 rather than a current guarantee; given a liked file moved into another folder while Otos was closed, the launch scan moves its row and the like follows; given a copy whose original was then deleted, the copy is a new row; given rename events for an old and a new path, the row moves with its like and no full scan runs; identity stays `(library_root_id, relative_path)`.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStorePersistence.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/CatalogModels.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogSchema.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogScannedFileReader.swift`

- **ARC-034** Byte-reading work shall consult `FileMaterialization` immediately before byte access so it never accidentally faults in a dataless file. [verified]
  - Scanners shall read only filesystem facts, while every deliberate provider download shall be submitted to the shared `MaterializationCoordinator`.
  - `FileMaterialization` shall own only one-URL detection/request/arrival-wait mechanics (arrival is the vnode's attribute change through an event-only descriptor; the provider is re-asked on a schedule because it acknowledges nothing), and consumers shall retain their decode/enrichment responsibilities.
  - `MediaEnricher` shall perform fresh gates immediately before SHA-1 hashing, after hashing before media metadata parsing, and immediately before Quick Look thumbnail generation (the `.removableVolume` path that skips hashing gates before media metadata parsing instead).
  - Import recovery shall perform the same live gate before hashing an ambiguous final.
  Example: Given a dataless cloud file, scanning does not download it; given preview or enrichment needs bytes, it checks live status, submits one typed materialization request, and rechecks status before reading; given provider eviction between enrichment phases, later byte phases return `.notMaterialized` without reading; given a dataless recovery final, no hash is attempted until deliberate materialization succeeds.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/CatalogScanner.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/FileMaterialization.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/MaterializationCoordinator.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/PreviewImageLoader.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/EnrichmentCoordinator.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/MediaEnricher.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/MediaImportExecutor.swift`

- **ARC-035** Otos shall keep media metadata (EXIF and similar embedded content, which requires reading bytes) distinct from filesystem facts (URL resource values readable without materialization), and the catalog shall store only filesystem facts. The word "metadata" and the Metadata instrumentation category mean media metadata. [verified]
  Example: Given the current catalog, it stores name, size, dates, and content type from resource values and no EXIF or embedded media metadata; given a doc or category says "metadata", it refers to byte-requiring media metadata.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStorePersistence.swift`

- **ARC-036** FileMaterialization shall report transient SF_DATALESS status without reading bytes, and MaterializationCoordinator shall bracket each coalesced physical transfer with exactly one Store known-materialization suppression window. [verified]
  - Only measured allowlisted regular-file hydration-shaped events shall suppress; create/remove/rename/directory/dropped/root shapes shall always reconcile.
  Example: Given multiple consumers request one identity, one provider operation and one suppression window occur; given success/failure the window ends once with the matching outcome; given a slow transfer the active window remains until completion.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/FileMaterialization.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/MaterializationCoordinator.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/KnownMaterializationSuppression.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`

- **ARC-037** Catalog display order shall be produced by Store-owned indexed SQLite queries over persisted sort facts and generated best values rather than Projection-local full-asset sorting. [verified]
  - File-name sort shall use a persisted numeric-aware name_sort_key.
  - A stack shall order its files by their paths' numeric-aware natural key (`MediaSortKey.naturalKey`), made in Swift when the stack is built (v71 removed the stored `sort_key`).
  - A whole-catalog `MediaSortKey` shall separate library-root and relative-path segments with U+0001, never an embedded NUL.
  Example: Given assets named `img2.jpg` and `img10.jpg`, the name sort key orders `img2` before `img10`; given Store returns ordered IDs, SQLite orders by descriptor-selected columns (`best_capture_local`, `name_sort_key`, or `byte_size`) rather than Projection sorting localized names or full asset values on every publication; given fallback enrichment has no visible priority candidates, Store claims newest best-date candidates with stable path tie-breakers instead of path-order `sort_key`; given an asset sort key is made, it separates library-root and relative-path segments with U+0001 rather than U+0000.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Models/CatalogModels.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`

- **ARC-038** If a scan batch re-observes identical visible facts (name, content type, size, and the `createdAt`/`contentModifiedAt` dates at whole-second resolution), then the Store shall not notify the projection, so re-scanning an unchanged library publishes nothing. [verified]
  - A scan pass shall compare each observed file with what the catalog held for its scope as the pass began (`ScanBaseline`, read once on a background lane, holding each row's facts as one SHA-256 of their bytes, `ScanFactValues.digest`, so a 160,000-row root's baseline holds 32 bytes a row beside its path rather than five strings, 127 MB) and send only differing files to the writer, so a pass over an unchanged scope takes no write transaction for its batches or its companions.
  - Dates shall be part of the key because Date sort and enrichment priority fall back to filesystem dates, and shall be compared at whole-second resolution so the SQLite `Double` round-trip epsilon cannot defeat the suppression.
  Example: Given a re-scan where every observed file's name, content type, size, and whole-second dates match the cataloged row, no batch or companion write transaction runs and `recordScannedBatch` does not call `notifyCatalogChanged`; given one file's modification second changed, exactly one write transaction changes exactly that row; given a file whose creation or modification second changes, the batch updates the row and notifies so descriptor order and enrichment priority can refresh; given a real 25k library with unchanged dates, 31 of 33 re-scan batches were suppressed and projection publications fell from 23 to 6; given two rows' facts, their digests are equal exactly when every fact is byte for byte, a missing value apart from an empty one and no two facts run together.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogSQLTypes.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStorePersistence.swift`

- **ARC-039** After a completed re-observation, the catalog database shall always mirror the latest scanned filesystem facts, including createdAt and contentModifiedAt. [verified]
  Example: Given a re-scan re-observes a file with unchanged name, content type, and size but a changed modification second, the scan upsert updates the SQLite row; because filesystem dates feed Date fallback ordering and enrichment priority, that whole-second date change republishes (ARC-038).
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`

- **ARC-040** Filesystem scans shall catalog only regular files whose NSURLContentTypeKey conforms to UTType.image or UTType.movie. Nil or non-conforming types are skipped at scan time, summarized in catalog.scan.skippedNonMedia diagnostics, and removed from the catalog on the next completed reconciliation when they were previously stored. [verified]
  Example: Given a library root contains photo.jpg, clip.mp4, notes.txt, and track.mp3, the completed scan catalogs only the image and video files; given a previously cataloged notes.txt row exists and a completed scan re-observes only photo.jpg, the text row is deleted.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/MediaEligibility.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogScanner.swift`

- **ARC-041** While the app is running, when filesystem changes occur under a saved available library root, the Store shall schedule automatic reconciliation for that root using typed dirty scopes, acted on as events arrive by one pass per root that coalesces events landing while it runs. [verified]
  - An event's path shall be read in the catalog's spelling, so a root under `/var`, `/tmp` or `/etc`, which FSEvents reports under `/private`, matches its own events.
  - Supported regular-file FSEvents shall reconcile only that path.
  - Trustworthy descendant directory/`MustScanSubDirs` events shall reconcile that subtree.
  - Directory rename events shall escalate to full-root reconciliation.
  - A remove-shaped directory event whose path is absent under a still-reachable root shall reconcile that subtree as an empty successful observation (purging catalog rows and companions under the boundary).
  - Dropped, unknown, root-changed, symlink, and other unsafe coverage shall fall back to full-root reconciliation.
  - Events that arrive while a scan/reconcile lease owns the root shall retain their file/subtree/root granularity in the next dirty generation instead of forcing an unconditional full-root rescan.
  - Otos's own `.otos-import-staging` transient files, written into the watched destination root while importing, shall never schedule reconciliation or advance occupancy.
  - Every independent namespace mutation shall advance the root's process-local epoch before its Store write and retain typed dirty scopes.
  - If a scan's captured epoch is no longer current, then the scan may upsert its observed facts but shall not delete missing media or companions.
  - An event's path shall map to its root-relative name by text alone, never by whether the path is a directory now, so a file that became a directory reconciles the row it had.
  - A single-file reconcile shall act on one observation of the path, read through the scanner boundary off the Store actor, and write only if, immediately before its write, the root's epoch is unchanged since its dirt was taken and no namespace lease holds the root; otherwise it shall write nothing and put that path and every path it had not reached back as dirt.
  - Recording an independent namespace mutation shall request reconciliation in the same step.
  - One Store-owned reconcile loop, woken by a revision-counted request, shall drain every dirty root: an event pass, a lease release, a namespace mutation, settlement and the integrity scheduler each request a pass, and the Store reports quiescence only when every request is covered and no dirt remains.
  - Whenever the single filesystem scan lease clears, the Store shall revisit every dirty root before starting the next queued full-root scan so retained dirt cannot be stranded behind another root's scan.
  - A Store-owned integrity scheduler shall mark each library root `.root` dirty once when 24 hours have elapsed since its last successful full-root reconciliation (a completed walk, or a launch replay its journal vouched for).
  - Each root shall have one stream, which replays its history at launch and keeps delivering while the app runs (ARC-025).
  - No manual scan UI is added.
  Example: Given a regular-file create event under `A`, Store reads that path's filesystem facts and upserts the row without scanning `A`; given a directory event under `A/2026/03`, Store enumerates only that subtree and does not delete sibling `A/2026/03b`; given a removed directory under a reachable root, rows and companions under that subtree are purged without requiring a later full-root scan; given a renamed directory, a full-root rescan prevents duplicate old/new path rows; given a dropped event under `A`, one full-root rescan for `A` runs after the quiet period; given a safe file event while a scan is active for `A` and another root is queued, Store accumulates `.file` dirt and settles it when the lease clears before or as part of revisiting all dirty roots; given an import/reorganization settles while a full scan is active, the stale scan cannot erase the settled destination; given a file reconcile observed a path missing and is suspended while undo puts the file back and restores its row and overlay, the reconcile defers and the row keeps its overlay; given a `.otos-import-staging` event arrives, no reconcile runs and no occupancy bump fires; given 24h since the last successful full-root scan, Store schedules one integrity root reconciliation without duplicating active/pending work.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/CatalogFilesystemWatcher.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/FilesystemEventFlags.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/FilesystemReconciler.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/FilesystemDirtyScope.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogScanner.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/MediaImportExecutor.swift`

- **ARC-043** The catalog schema shall be one baseline migration (`v1_catalog_baseline`, created from `CatalogSchema`), and later schema changes shall be additive GRDB migrations registered after it. [verified]
  - There is no upgrade from catalogs written before the baseline.
  - Destructive schema reset is forbidden.
  - If a catalog's applied migrations include identifiers this build does not register (written by a newer Otos, or from before the baseline) or its user tables exist without known migration identifiers, then the catalog shall refuse to open without writing to the database file; the window shows a typed incompatible empty state, loads no roots, and runs no scans.
  - `CatalogMediaEmptyState.catalogIncompatible` shall carry the typed `CatalogSchemaCompatibility` value itself, not a pre-rendered string — `CatalogMediaEmptyStatePresentation.swift`'s pure mapper (beside `ImportPlanFooter.swift`'s, for the same reason) is the one place that turns it into the user-facing message, not the ViewModel.
  - Journal and settlement write transactions shall escalate to pragma for their commit.
  Example: Given a new catalog, it records only the baseline migration and holds exactly the baseline tables; given a database with an unknown migration identifier — a future one or a pre-baseline one — and existing rows/thumbnails, CatalogStore.start throws unsupportedCatalogSchema(.writtenByOtherOtos), the rows and thumbnails remain on disk, and the ViewModel surfaces .catalogIncompatible.

- **ARC-044** The Store shall expose import-history queries over `import_batch_unix_ms` (nonempty batches newest-first, and asset IDs for one batch) without a separate import-batch table. [verified]

  Example: Given two imports started in one millisecond, their batches differ; given three assets stamped with two batch IDs, `importBatches()` returns the newer batch first with correct counts and `assetIDs(inImportBatch:)` returns only that batch's survivors; given the user selects an Imports row, ordered IDs, both asset counts, and the offered filter options are all fixed-library assets in that batch under the current sort — so the filter menu offers only kinds and formats the batch contains, and a batch emptied by the filter reports `.noFilterMatches` rather than an empty library; given every fixed library is unreachable, a batch scope reports `.noAvailableMedia`; given a published-entry count differs from durable batch rows, execution reports entry failure.

- **ARC-045** Otos shall inventory recognized useful companions (`.xmp` / `.srt`, case-insensitive) in `media_companion`, paired by stem key — one folder and one stem compared as the volume compares names (`FilesystemNamespaceSemantics.stemKey`, ARC-056; ADR 0052) — (RAW+JPEG prefers raw; otherwise natural-name first; unpaired retained), reconciled on full scan and targeted FSEvents, and they shall never enter media queries or enrichment.
