- **ARC-046** Import planning shall produce an immutable ready plan from Store snapshots plus live destination occupancy with no UI or file mutation. [verified]
  - `MediaTransferPlanner` shall be pure and single-pass: `destinationFolders(for:)` derives every folder a plan can touch from source facts plus policy before any I/O, `FilesystemNamespaceSemantics.enumerateNamespace` reads exactly those folders once, and `planImport` decides every candidate (base name and each suffix retry) from that namespace without further observation.
  - Destination freeness shall use `FilesystemNamespaceSemantics` (not catalog trust alone), and absence shall be claimed only for an enumerated folder — an unreadable folder blocks its candidates instead of reading as free, and a folder a file makes impossible (the file holds its name or an ancestor's) blocks its candidates as `.destinationFolderOccupied` instead of being retried.
  - A selected regular-file candidate shall be preexisting only when its live byte size and filesystem-precision mtime match the shared expected destination mtime.
  - Naming shall be `ImportNaming`, the one owner Reorganize shares.
  - Original-name imports shall preserve each physical member's exact filename and extension, a companion's extension spelling included.
  - Date/time naming shall give a companion its kind's extension and use the content type's Apple-preferred filename extension, preserving the source extension only when the type has no usable media preference.
  - Import-plan staleness shall key on two per-root revisions, applied by role.
  - `namespaceRevision` — which paths the root occupies — shall advance only when the occupied media namespace actually changes (asset added/removed/renamed/resized, companion inventory, import/reorg settlement, foreign occupant), and shall invalidate a plan reading that root as source **or** destination.
  - `namingRevision` — what Otos would call the root's assets — shall advance when an enrichment write changes `capture_wall_compact` on an import source (a card or camera), and shall invalidate a plan reading that root as **source only**, because the source capture wall is what the date/time destination name is derived from; on a library the write advances only `planRevision`, which reorganization reads, and on an Immich account nothing.
  - The Store shall wake the import coordinator only for a root an import plans against and only when its namespace or naming revision moved (`CatalogStore.recordNamingChange`, `LibraryRootKind.isImportPlanned`).
  - An enrichment write on a *destination* renames nothing on disk and shall never be read by `fetchDestinationOccupants` (`relative_path`, `byte_size`, `content_modified_at_unix` only), so it never invalidates a plan copying into that root — planning settles while a large cloud destination keeps enriching.
  - Identical rescans shall advance neither revision.
  - The Store shall publish a lossless `RootPlanningChange` stream carrying both revisions; the coordinator shall subscribe before its first refresh, so no change falls between the two, and shall apply each by role and treat `planRevision`-only wakes (no-op rescans) as no-ops.
  - Events during an active scan shall retain typed dirty scopes in the next dirty generation without planning-churn deferrals.
  Example: Given a source with capture-wall facts and an empty destination, the planner emits `.copy` under year/month + `YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS` naming with Apple's preferred extension; given original naming, every media and companion member retains its exact source name; given matching live size and expected mtime at the selected path, disposition is `.skipPreexisting` even without a catalog row; given a foreign occupant or mismatched/unknown live fact, the bundle advances suffix; given sources spanning three capture months, `destinationFolders(for:)` returns exactly those three folders and every chosen destination — suffix retries included — falls inside them; given a destination folder that could not be enumerated, its entries are `.blocked` and no copy is planned; given a destination folder that does not exist yet, every candidate in it is free; given no usable date, the entry is `.blocked(.noUsableDate)`; given repeated no-op `planRevision` bumps on the destination, the ready plan stays ready without a rebuild, while a real namespace bump forces a replan; given the destination library enriches and its `namingRevision` advances repeatedly, the ready plan keeps its generation and is not rebuilt, while a destination `namespaceRevision` bump does rebuild it; given the source's `namingRevision` advances, the plan is rebuilt because the copy's name is derived from that fact; given safe file events while a scan lease is active, Store accumulates `.file` dirt and settles after the lease without an unconditional full-root follow-up.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/MediaTransferPlanner.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/ImportDestinationDate.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/FilesystemNamespaceSemantics.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/MediaOperationCoordinator.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/FilesystemReconciler.swift`

- **ARC-047** Where a removable source is attached, Otos shall give it per-source plans/state and let it queue Import. [verified]
  - Plans shall revalidate occupancy before publication and cancel on detach.
  - A change recorded while a build runs — a revision, destination or preference change — shall never be cleared by that build's completion: a plan request is a counter (`planRequested`/`planCovered`) that only taking a build up covers, so the session re-plans after it.
  - Import intents (destination, session preferences, Import, Cancel, Try Again, preference changes) shall reach the coordinator through one lossless ordered stream with one consumer in the view model, so "change the destination, then Import" imports into the new destination, and Import returns when the intent is applied rather than when the copy ends, so Cancel is never held behind it.
  - A Cancel shall reach the run's executor however early it comes: the coordinator makes the run's `MediaImportExecutor` and registers it in the synchronous step that starts the run, before its source-read bracket or its hop onto the executor, the cancel is set in the same step as the transition that asked for it and never cleared, and a run whose `execute` begins after it ends stopped by the user with nothing copied, its task left running so what already landed settles (only a removed source cancels the task too).
  - Execution progress shall travel in one per-run newest-value stream that is drained before the run's result is reported, so no sample follows the settlement.
  - Session state shall change only in `ImportSessionMachine.handle(_:)`, a synchronous transition fed build, execution, refresh and intent events, each result dropped unless its typed identity still owns the session.
  - Device (MTP) enumeration batches and completion shall advance the same root occupancy revision as a filesystem scan, so a plan built before live device inventory landed is marked dirty and rebuilt once it does — not left permanently stale.
  - A published plan shall survive the rebuilds that replace it: the session keeps the last plan it proved and keeps presenting it while a newer build over the same proven set is in flight, so a refresh never reads as a first check — except when the rebuild's inputs changed.
  - After a run has settled, the plan it executed from shall be dropped because that run made it false.
  - Whenever the destination changes — a pop-up pick, or a default following a library reorder — the held plan and the settled outcome shall be dropped because their counts were measured against the previous library — the bar returns to Checking (with its naming count, when a metadata wait was the last thing it proved) until the plan for the new library lands, and never presents the old plan under the new library's name or offers an Import whose number the click would not honour.
  - A run's outcome shall be a recorded fact, not a phase: completion, stop and failure are each stored on the session and survive the post-settlement rebuild; the phase says only what the session is doing now.
  - A failed run shall state the count its executor published before it failed, never a later catalog read.
  - A proven plan shall carry the library it was proven for and the run that built it, and a running import shall carry its own destination, progress and batch proof, so name claims and settlement are about the library the plan was built for, and a run releases that library — not the source's destination at settlement — to the rebuilds other sources owe it.
  - If a build could not read a destination folder, then it shall be deferred and retried with a bounded backoff while the last proven plan keeps presenting; it is never published as a plan (ADR 0019).
  - A plan shall not be presented while naming facts are pending — rows enrichment has not read are not described by the held plan, and reporting its count would under-report them.
  - The session shall keep the last **naming progress** it proved and present that across a rebuild, so a re-plan during preparation never drops the bar's count or its determinate track; only an attachment that has never reported progress draws a countless Preparing.
  - The session shall keep one last proof — a plan, a settled run or a naming count (`SourceSession.lastProven`) — and each replaces the one before it, so a count from before a plan is never shown after it, not even while the card is read again and the held plan cannot be offered.
  - A source's plan shall be offered as Ready and Import shall become enabled only once `ImportSourceReadiness` (ADR 0024) proves the source's own live scan/enumeration has settled to idle — never while it is still scanning/enumerating or unreachable, and never as a provably-empty card until the current plan is guaranteed to reflect the settled occupancy.
  - A plan whose build was taken up while the source was still being read shall never be offered or run: once the source settles it is rebuilt, whether or not the read moved a revision and whether the source settled before or after that build finished; `op=import.attachToReady` measures only a plan that can be offered.
  - Import shall use MediaImportExecutor, which never refuses a run for want of destination space — it copies entries in plan order up to `FreeSpaceFloor.bytes` and stops as a designed, resumable outcome (ADR 0027) — deletion settles through the journal; auto-eject occurs only after complete success.
  - The post-import auto-eject shall fire the ViewModel's eject intent — the one the sidebar's eject control fires — so it drains Otos's own readers, retries a busy volume, and surfaces a failure the same way (2026-09-13: it had skipped the drain and dropped the outcome).
  - The import destination shall be remembered per removable source (`destinationBySource`, the machine's one record of it, loaded before the first attachment is seen and changed only through one writer) and read back on every new attachment, including a reattach of a source Otos has already seen — an explicit pop-up pick or a still-tracked default alike resumes exactly where it left off.
  - On first sight of a source, or when a remembered library is no longer available, the destination shall fall back to the first available library in the user's own Settings → Libraries order (`library_root.sort_index`) and start tracking it.
  - A destination still on that tracked default shall keep following reorders and reattachments — a change made while a run executes is held until that run settles, then applied — while an explicit pick survives an ordinary refresh until it actually becomes unavailable.
  - The Store shall assign each removable attachment a process-local generation and the coordinator shall publish it with the source presentation.
  - A source's presentation shall exist only while its card is attached: on detach its outcome — whatever it was — is logged (`op=import.outcome phase=settled`) and discarded, never kept for another row.
  - The executing task shall be owned and drained after detach, so its eventual journal count can settle the old batch without mutating a replacement attachment at the same path.
  Example: Given a source attaches with a library, it reaches Ready; given a Cancel delivered after a run started and before its executor's `execute` began, the run ends stopped by the user and copies nothing; given a real occupancy change forces a rebuild of an already-ready source, every presentation published during that rebuild is still Ready rather than falling back to preparing; given a plan was proven or a run settled before the card gained rows whose naming facts are pending, every presentation published during the metadata wait — rebuilds and stale builds included — is the naming count, never Ready or Done; given a plan proven after a metadata wait, a rebuild while the card is read again shows Checking with no count; given attach after startup, planning stream reaches Ready; given zero new items with delete, source items are deleted; given execution fails, source remains; given blocked entries remain, cleanup is withheld; given a stale build after detach, no presentation appears; given a card is detached during or after a run, no presentation for it remains; given the user reorders libraries while a card is attached and still on the default, the destination follows the new first library; given the reorder happens while the card's import is executing, the running copy keeps its destination and the re-plan after settlement follows the new first library; given a destination was picked via the pop-up, it survives a reorder that doesn't affect its own availability; given a source with an explicit or default destination is detached and later reattaches, it resumes at that same remembered destination instead of resetting to the first library.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/MediaOperationCoordinator.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/ImportSessionMachine.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/ImportSourceReadiness.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/ImportExecuting.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/ImportPlanBuilder.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/MediaImportExecutor.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/ImportPlanFooter.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/ImportBarPresentation.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelMediaOperations.swift`

- **ARC-048** Filesystem import shall journal each copy in `active_file_operation`, stage via `copyfile(3)` with byte progress/cancel on the copy queue (`CopyfileTransfer.offCooperativePool`, a dispatch queue of its own, never the cooperative pool, which eight blocked copies fill on an eight-core Mac), hash the completed primary staging file in the same eight-worker import operation, durably persist the digest plus `.readyToPublish` before exclusive publication, stamp destination mtime from capture-wall or source mtime, publish with the exclusive-publication ladder (`renamex_np(RENAME_EXCL)` / hard-link / check-then-act), durably persist `.publishedPendingSettlement` after the rename, and settle the digest plus actual destination size/mtime atomically with journal clearance under pragma. [verified]
  - If a journal update or delete mutates zero rows, then it shall fail.
  - Every staging file an entry creates in the library — the primary's and each companion's — shall be journaled on the entry before the file exists, so no staging file is ever unnamed by a record and no directory sweep exists.
  - When an entry ends without publishing, it shall remove its staging files and then its row; if a file will not go, then the row shall be kept in phase `abandoned` (not work in flight) for the next launch.
  - Settlement shall clear the row.
  - A landed entry's card file shall be erased on its own settle (ADR 0027) by one ordered consumer per run, under the card's namespace lease and journaled in `active_source_erase` like the sweep; a card another owner holds keeps the file for the end-of-run sweep, which runs under its own lease (APP-032).
  - `MediaImportExecutor` shall own one run's filesystem lifecycle; `MediaImportRecovery` shall own earlier processes' journals and run once at `MediaOperationCoordinator.start()`, before the coordinator runs any import, so a running import is never treated as a crash to recover.
  - Recovery shall judge each row only after the Store's initial load has registered persisted roots; it removes every companion staging file the row names (recovery never publishes a companion), then follows the phase/final/staging matrix for the primary: final-plus-staging and exclusive destination races are foreign, final-only recovery requires size/integer-second mtime/SHA-1 proof, dataless or unknown finals retain the journal for deliberate materialization, and published finals without a journal row are left for the next plan.
  - `Store` shall own semantic durable transitions and atomic catalog settlement, and shall perform no import filesystem cleanup.
  - Each planned companion shall be copied through the same byte path into its own hidden staging file beside its destination, after the primary's hash, and published exclusively after the primary; only a companion that landed is recorded with the primary, with the facts of the file that landed.
  - If a companion did not land (copy refused, destination taken), then it shall not be recorded, its source shall never be erased, and its item counts as not copied (an incomplete entry failure the re-plan offers again); a cancel or a full volume during a companion stops the entry like the primary.
  Example: Given a ready plan with one `.copy` entry, Import creates a journal row, copies and hashes a hidden adjacent staging file, durably records readiness, publishes exclusively, durably records pending settlement, settles a canonical digest and `import_batch_unix_ms`, and clears the journal; given final already exists, publish returns foreign-occupant and the entry does not overwrite; given a `copying` journal with staging after crash, recovery deletes staging and clears the row, including when coordinator start reaches the Store before it has loaded its roots; given final and staging both exist, recovery removes staging only and records foreign; given a matching journaled final-only file, recovery settles it, while a mismatch retains the source; given a published journal with a digest, recovery settles it only after ownership proof; given an `.xmp` companion that copies, it lands beside its primary, is recorded paired to it, and Erase removes its source; given a companion whose copy fails or whose destination was taken, the primary lands, the companion is not recorded or overwritten, its source survives Erase, the run reports the item as not copied and incomplete, and no staging file remains; given a run while another run's entry is in flight, the other entry's row and staging file are untouched; given another owner holding the card during a run with Erase, every copy lands and no card file is erased, neither on settle nor in the sweep; given a journaled companion staging file after a crash, recovery removes it and settles the primary; given a staging file that cannot be removed, the row stays `abandoned` until a later recovery removes both; given every worker's copy held, other async work still runs.

- **ARC-050** Library reorganization planning shall be a pure, non-mutating planner that applies the import naming policy through the same `ImportNaming` owner import uses. [verified]
  - Naming bundles (one stem key, ARC-045 — a RAW and its JPEG) shall share one date and one collision suffix.
  - Suffixes shall be the import policy's `_001`, `_002`, and companions shall be named after their primary.
  - The runner shall read exactly the folders `destinationFolders(for:)` returns, once, and the planner shall decide every candidate from that namespace in one pass.
  - A bundle shall stay where it is when it is undated, when a file holds its folder's name or an ancestor's (`folderOccupied`), when its folder could not be read (`folderUnreadable`), when it is already named as the policy names it, or when no suffix is free.
  - Staying members shall keep their names and blocked members shall never enter the move graph.
  - A moving bundle shall take the first suffix whose names are not kept by a staying bundle, not taken by an earlier bundle, and not held by a file the catalog does not know.
  - All path comparisons shall use the volume name key.
  - Renames shall be ordered so none lands on an occupied slot, and a cycle or a case-only relabel shall step aside through one temporary name in its own folder, built from the member's catalog path.
  - If duplicate final lookup keys occur, then they shall make the plan inconsistent and it shall never be run.
  Example: Given two files that only differ by case on a case-insensitive volume, the rename steps aside through a stable temporary name; given a .HIF item identified as HEIF under date/time naming, the destination extension is .heif; given two sources competing for a name a staying file holds, they take `_001` and `_002`; given a RAW and its JPEG whose name is taken, both take the same suffix; given an undatable item, only that media is `.blocked(.noUsableDate)`; given a file holding the destination folder's name, the item is `.blocked(.folderOccupied)` and nothing moves; given a swap, one member steps aside once.

- **ARC-051** Library reorganization execution shall journal each dependency component in normalized `active_reorganization_*` tables before the first move, hold one typed root reorganization lease for the run (deferring scans/events; dirty-only reconcile at release), execute same-volume no-overwrite steps with forward/ambiguous recovery, and settle identities with a set-based two-phase private-key remap that preserves overlay columns and thumbnail path references, recording one pending `CatalogIdentityRemapBatch` per settled component in the settlement transaction. [verified]
  - A component shall be atomic against the library: if one cannot finish (destination taken, folder unavailable, move refused, journal or settlement write failed), then it shall be rolled back — completed steps moved back newest first, folders it created removed once empty, journal deleted — before the lease is released, and the run shall stop there with earlier components settled.
  - If a rollback cannot restore a file, then it shall mark the component blocked and keep the journal and the lease.
  - From the moment the Store loads, every root with a journaled component shall be held under the reorganization lease, so nothing scans, reconciles, imports into or trashes from a half-moved library before its recovery.
  - Startup recovery, run once by the composition root after the Store starts, shall adopt that hold and resume every journal: `prepared`/`moving` run forward and settle, `filesystemComplete` settles, `rollingBack`/`blocked` roll back; a component that reaches an end state releases its lease.
  - A run shall execute only the plan revision the user confirmed; a changed library shall return the new plan for confirmation and move nothing.
  - `ReorganizationExecutor` shall own directory, move and rollback mechanics; `LibraryReorganizationRunner` shall own execution and startup recovery decisions; `Store` shall own scan-exclusion leases, semantic durable transitions, and atomic catalog settlement.
  Example: Given a swap component, the journal records the temporary hop and settlement remaps both media rows without PK collision; given a .willMove crash with source absent and destination present, recovery treats the move as done and settles the component; given a component stops on a taken destination, its moved file returns, its created folder is removed and its journal is deleted; given the original name is taken before rollback, the component stays journaled as blocked; given a run stops at its second component, the first stays settled with its pending remap and the lease is released; given a run confirmed against another revision, nothing moves and the current plan is returned; given a lease is held, filesystem events mark dirty and do not reconcile until release.

  - Identity remaps shall reach the window only through the catalog: settlement records a pending batch, every ordered-ID read returns pending batches from the same read transaction, and `CatalogWindowViewModel.applyState` carries GridSelection, loupe/compare focus and TileContentStore (preserving already-painted thumbnails) across each batch once, before comparing the new order with the old, then acknowledges it so the Store deletes it.
  - If a run cannot start or stops partway, then Otos shall show an alert in user vocabulary (items, files, folders) naming the cause and whether the rest of the library was left as it was, titled by what happened as a title-case fragment (DES-037): `Couldn’t Reorganize Library` when it could not start, `Reorganization Stopped` when it stopped.
  Example: Given a painted tile's identity remaps, the thumbnail remains visible under the new ID; given a selected/focused asset remaps, selection and focus follow and are not pruned; given a state carries a batch already applied, nothing changes; given no fixed library is selected, the Library menu command and the Reorganize action are both disabled; given the confirmation dialog appears, its summary text names only items and a can't-process count, never the planner's internal vocabulary.; given a run could not start, the alert is titled `Couldn’t Reorganize Library`; given it stopped, `Reorganization Stopped`, and its message does not repeat that it stopped as its opening.

- **ARC-053** Import settlement shall verify a completed batch against durable `import_batch_unix_ms` rows and shall give the copy each product separately (ADR 0009). [verified]
  - Import settlement shall give the copy the source row's metadata when its metadata stamp (`metadata_source_size`, `metadata_source_mtime`) names the size and mtime the import copied, and its rendering — thumbnail and preview — when its rendering stamp (`rendered_source_size`, `rendered_source_mtime`) does, the same freshness rule enrichment applies to every row.

  - The import shall never render or read for a product: failing a rendering, the source's thumbnail shall be carried as an unstamped provisional thumbnail, and a copy that settles owing a product shall be offered to enrichment before every other background row (`localBytesAwaitingProducts`) while its bytes are local.
  - Derived images shall be copied or promoted through the normal staged-promotion path, the destination shall be stamped with the digest the import computed from its staged copy, and each product's origin (`reused`, `provisional`, `owed`) shall be returned and logged.
  Example: Given an imported card or library source whose stamps match the copied size and mtime, the destination is settled with the source's metadata and rendering, its own digest, and needs no enrichment; given a card row with metadata but no rendering yet, the copy gets the metadata, owes the rendering, and is the first row the next background claim returns; given the source file changed after it was enriched, both products are owed; given durable batch count differs from published-entry count, the execution result reports entry failure.

- **ARC-056** Filesystem name equality shall follow the containing volume's probed case and Unicode-normalization capabilities through one `FilesystemNameKey` owner. [verified]
  - Catalog reconciliation, import namespace/reservation planning, and reorganization shall retain physical spelling while comparing derived keys; a respelling shall update the existing overlay row rather than purging it.
  - Every write of a row's path, a scan's, an import's or a reorganization's, shall write that path's key with it, made with the root's stored capabilities (`case_sensitive_names`, read in the writing transaction by an import's settlement and a reorganization's; no key-making write takes a default).
  - A scan shall rewrite a key it finds out of date, so a row is found only by the key of the path it holds.
  Example: Given a case-only respelling on a case-insensitive volume, the catalog keeps one asset and its overlay; given a case-sensitive destination, case-only names remain distinct; given import and reorganization planning, the same key rules prevent overwrite or duplicate claims; given a reorganized photo, a new file at the path it left is a new photo and the moved photo keeps its overlay; given a catalog with empty or stale keys, the v36 migration makes every key from its path; given an import of `2024/IMG_1.jpg` onto a case-sensitive root holding a Liked `2024/img_1.jpg`, the copy is a new photo and the existing one keeps its path and choice.

- **ARC-057** While any source row's naming fact is unproven — that is, enrichment has not yet read the file's metadata (`metadata_source_size`/`metadata_source_mtime` absent) and no capture wall is stored — import planning shall withhold a date-dependent plan. [verified]
  - "No capture wall" on an unread row means "not yet looked at", not "none exists": naming from the filesystem mtime there asserts a fact Otos has not established, and because the destination name is also the dedupe key, the next plan computes a different name, finds nothing at that path, and copies again.
  - A row shall be `.terminal` — plan now — once a capture wall is stored, or once enrichment has looked and found none, which makes the mtime a *proven* fallback rather than an assumed one.
  - Readiness shall wait on the metadata stamp alone, never on the rendering or digest (ADR 0009).
  - The naming question shall stay derived separately from the metadata staleness predicate, which answers whether enrichment should re-run: a stored capture wall is terminal even when the predicate can never mark the row current.
  - A build that decides to wait shall revalidate against live rows before parking, because a batch landing mid-build is not replayed by the catalog-change wake; and when a catalog change is handled after the build was taken up — a batch landing after that live read — the parked session shall plan again at once.
  - A source file whose bytes could not be opened shall never be treated as pending: it can be neither enriched nor copied, so it does not hold the offer open, the plan blocks it, and the bar surfaces it as an attention item rather than silently skipping or endlessly retrying it.
  - The gate shall apply to every source kind including MTP cameras: a camera's bytes are staged locally precisely so they can be enriched, and `ICCameraFile`'s own dates are absolute instants that cannot yield a local wall clock, so a camera import offered before enrichment carries the same fabricated names as a card's.
  - A camera item's scanned modification date shall be taken capture-first, since the modification-first precedence is end-of-recording for video.
  - When enrichment changes the capture-wall fact, it shall advance the root's `namingRevision`, which causes a re-plan of every session reading that root as its **source** — the capture wall names the copy.
  - It shall not advance `namespaceRevision`, so a plan copying *into* an enriching root is untouched (ARC-046).
  - A row naming from mtime shall use no fabricated sentinel.
  Example: Given a source row enrichment has not read, the coordinator publishes metadata waiting rather than Ready even though the row carries a filesystem mtime; given enrichment then reads it and finds no embedded date, the source reaches Ready and names from that mtime as a proven fallback; given enrichment supplies a capture wall for a row whose mtime is absent — a row the staleness predicate can never mark current — the source reaches Ready instead of waiting forever; given an enrichment batch lands while a build is in flight, the build revalidates against live rows and rebuilds instead of parking on a wake that will never arrive; given the batch lands after that read, the session parks and plans again in the same step; given a source file whose bytes cannot be opened, the plan blocks that entry as unreadable, the offer is not withheld on its account, and it is neither counted as copied nor as already present; given capture-wall enrichment changes, the next plan uses the updated name.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Models/MediaOperationModels.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/MediaOperationGateway.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/MediaOperationCoordinator.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/ImportSessionMachine.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/ImportNaming.swift`

- **ARC-058** A pending Move to Trash shall be a durable fact on the asset's own catalog row (`media_asset.pending_trash_batch_unix_ms`, schema v20), not a second table or an in-memory set. [verified]
  - Every user-facing media query shall exclude pending rows through the shared scope clause, and import-history counts shall exclude them.
  - The row and its overlay shall be kept until the file is proven gone, and a scan that sees a file vanish that the queue has not moved shall end the intent by deleting the row.
  - Once the queue moves the file to the system Trash, the row shall be kept — still hidden, no longer offered to the queue — with the reported Trash path (`media_asset.trash_path`, schema v10), shall never be claimed by enrichment, and shall be purged by a scan only once the Trash no longer holds the file (`ENOENT`).
  - When a file is seen again at the row's path (Finder's Put Back, after a relaunch too, or undo), the row shall show again with its overlay, and clearing a pending mark shall never show such a row.
  - The trash bar's Cancel shall withdraw every item still pending, read from these rows, so it works after a relaunch.
  - The queue that drains pending rows shall treat a failed Store read as unknown, not empty: a pass whose pending-item read fails publishes no queue state and retries on a timer; a download size that cannot be read is unknown, not zero; and an item the user took back (restore, withdraw, immediate delete, undo of a pending request) while a pass held it in its snapshot is never moved afterwards.
  - Shutdown shall end the queue's work: a pass suspended when it lands starts no download and arms no timer, so no download outlives shutdown.
  Example: Given an item is marked pending, grid and count queries omit it while its file and row exist; given the mark is cleared, it reappears; given pending rows and a new coordinator, the rows are found and drained; given the pending-item read fails during a pass, the queue state the user last saw stands; given the user restores an item while a pass is partway through its snapshot, that item is not moved; given shutdown lands while a pass is suspended in its pending-item read after the cancelled download cleared its slot, no download starts and every started one has ended when shutdown returns; given items moved to the Trash, then a relaunch where one was put back and another emptied from the Trash, the put-back one shows with its choice and edit, the emptied one is purged, and one still in the Trash stays hidden and unqueued; given a pending mark is cleared for an item already in the Trash, it does not show; given pending items from an earlier run, Cancel withdraws them (`OtosTests/CatalogStoreTrashPutBackTests.swift`).
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStorePersistence.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogQueryGateway.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/MediaTrashModels.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/MediaTrashCoordinator.swift`

- **ARC-062** Otos shall treat a RAW and the camera's processed still of one shot as one stack (ADR 0052), derived and never stored: in one root and one folder, exactly two present stills sharing a stem key (ARC-045), one RAW and one processed image. [verified]
  - Any other count shall pair nothing, and a video shall never make or break one.
  - The Store shall read every candidate (each present RAW and processed image in a folder holding a present RAW and another present still, and every present burst still, whatever the scope or filter, `PhotoStackSQL`) in the same transaction as the ordered IDs, through `idx_media_asset_stack_candidate` (root, kind, folder; schema v33), and `PhotoStackRule` shall decide the stacks.
  - A scope that names its rows by a key (ARC-022) shall read its rows first, then only the candidates their stacks can be built from — their folders that can hold a pair, the stills of the bursts they and those folders' stills carry, and those stills' folders that can hold a pair, never walking another folder — and shall keep the stacks that hold one of its rows (`PhotoStackSQL.Span.rows`), the same stacks a read of every candidate gives those rows; held stacks of every candidate answer it too, and its own are never held.
  - A stack's key shall be the member the user made it most recently (`media_asset.stack_key_chosen_at_unix_ms`, schema v32, carried by moves and purged with its row), else the processed file.
  - An iPhone burst shall be a stack too: two or more frames in one root whose stills carry one burst identifier (`media_asset.burst_identifier`, schema v37, written with each metadata record and filled from the stored records); a pair whose file carries it is one frame, shown as the pair's key.
  - A camera's continuous-shooting frames shall never stack: the frame number MediaMetadata 0.9.5 reads from a Sony MakerNote (kept in the stored metadata record) says where a frame stands in its sequence, not which sequence, so once the user deletes a burst's leading frames nothing tells them from the burst before (ADR 0052).
  - Schema v46 drops v42's `continuous_shooting_frame` column and `idx_media_asset_burst_or_frame`, and iPhone bursts shall be read through `idx_media_asset_burst` again.
  - A burst's key shall be the frame the user made it most recently (`media_asset.burst_key_chosen_at_unix_ms`, apart from a pair's choice), else its first Liked frame, else its first frame in capture order.
  Example: Given a RAW and its JPEG beside a JPEG alone and a same-stem JPEG in another folder, one pair whose key is the JPEG; given a filter showing only RAWs, the RAW is still read as one of a pair; given Make Key Photo on the RAW and then its undo, the RAW is the key and then the JPEG again; given three stills with one burst identifier, one burst in capture order keyed by its first frame, or its first Liked frame, or the frame chosen last; given a pair whose JPEG carries the identifier, the pair is one frame; given a camera's frame 2 of one burst and frames 3 and 5 of a burst another day in another folder, and an intact burst numbered 1, 2, no burst at all; given the stacks read through the candidate index, no arm scans `media_asset` and none sorts the candidates; given the owner's shape, the read leaves out every RAW alone in its folder and builds exactly the stacks a read of every RAW folder's stills builds; given an import batch holding a JPEG whose RAW is not in it and a frame whose burst's other frame is in another folder, the batch reads five candidates and exactly the whole library's pair and burst for its rows, and no stack without a batch row; given a narrow scope's candidates, every step seeks by index.

- **ARC-063** The catalog's write-ahead log shall stay bounded under sustained writes with overlapping reads, and bounding it shall never wait on a reader (ADR 0002). [verified]
  - The writer's `journal_size_limit` shall be `CatalogStore.defaultWALByteBudget` (64 MiB).
  - After every bulk write — a scan batch, a device- or source-listing batch, an enrichment batch — the Store shall read the WAL file's size, and when it is larger than the budget it shall run a passive checkpoint on the writer, which copies every frame no reader still needs and neither waits for nor holds up a read.
  - If a checkpoint fails, then it shall be logged (`op=catalog.database.wal phase=checkpointFailed`) and retried by the next bulk write.
  Example: Given a bulk write leaves the WAL file within its budget, it is left as SQLite's own checkpoint keeps it, logged only when the file grew past the largest size logged since the last checkpoint (`phase=grew walBytes=`); given it is larger, the write checkpoints the log (`phase=checkpointed` with `copiedFrames` and `ms`); given a long background read in flight, that write, a window read and a user's write all finish before the read ends; given the read has ended, a later write leaves the file at most the budget.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStorePersistence.swift`

- **ARC-064** The window shall take in a publication at a cost in what changed, never in the library's size (ADR 0004, ADR 0005). [verified]
  - The grid's order, day buckets and stacks shall be one immutable `CatalogGridContent` shared by reference.
  - Projection, at publication and off the main actor, shall hand on the last publication's own content — and its own map, On This Day, duplicate groups, People, filter options and import batches — wherever a pass rebuilt them equal, and shall make the new content's position index.
  - An order made by an edit (a Move to Trash, a Put Back, the acts held on a read, a read of the same query published as an edit of the last) shall take its source's index with the edit applied, in the edit's cost, until the composed edit names as many positions as the order holds, and only a new query's order shall write one whole.
  - `CatalogWindowViewModel.state` shall notify its observers without comparing (a published state is a change by construction).
  - `applyState` shall tell a changed grid by the content's identity, prune the selection and reconcile loupe, compare and story focus by lookups in the content's index, carry a selection into stacks only when the content changed, and forget duplicate picks only when the groups changed.
  Example: Given a 158,000-tile republication that moved nothing the user sees, the apply keeps selection and focus and costs what a small one does; given an order that lost the focused item, focus moves to its nearest survivor; given an equal rebuilt content, the publication carries the last one's; given other days or stacks over the same order, it carries new content.

- **ARC-065** The window shall start its background owners only once it has applied its first grid, and their heavy loads shall run one at a time (ADR 0055). [verified]
  - `CatalogWindowViewModel` shall open `LoadAdmission` and start the owners in one order when it logs `op=launch.firstGrid`.
  - A People pass, a duplicates build, the search index's first load, an account's mirror pairing, the derived folder's launch look (`DerivedFolderLoad`) and each description request (`PhotoDescriptionWriter`, `BackgroundLoad.description`) shall each run inside `LoadAdmission.run`, which admits none before the first grid, at most one at a time, those asked before the first grid in `BackgroundLoad.launchRank` order (the first description after the others, before readying a search) and later ones in the order asked, and the load the current query's view needs (`BackgroundLoad.needed(by:)`) before the others, without stopping the one running, and beside a running description request, granted in the step it is wanted or asked.
  - A description request shall need no available share and be charged no cost: its grant, and a grant beside it, leave no load to blame.
  - A background heavy load shall be granted only while the room reads at least its requirement available, the warning line (32 %) plus its cost, from the first grant of it that raised the pressure.
  - Each beginning and end of the room's memory hold shall be logged with the loads running up to it (`op=load.admission phase=memoryHold held= running=`).
  - The background heavy load granted last when the background room's memory hold begins (ADR 0057) shall be logged (`op=load.admission phase=pressureRaised`) with the shares at its grant, the lowest since and at the hold, and its requirement, which becomes the largest of what it was, the line plus the grant's share less the lowest, and one above the grant's share, and never falls in the session; such a load shall wait behind every other waiting load except readying a search for the session.
  - While a load waits for its requirement, the room shall read the kernel on its look; an unread share shall admit a load.
  - A load the user waits on (`LoadWaiter.user`: a search query's or the intent to search's text-encoder load, `SearchQueryReader`; a People pass that covers a People act the user made, `PeopleAssignment.requestForUser`) shall be admitted at once, whatever the background room holds, beside the one running and ahead of the waiting ones, none of which starts while it runs.
  - When the user's act lands, a People pass already waiting shall be promoted to theirs (`LoadAdmission.promote`), so a face named shows its twins and neighbours assigned while the room holds.
  - The first grid's readying of search shall load the encoder inside its own turn.
  - The window's teardown shall refuse every waiting load.
  - Owners' tasks shall run at `TaskPriority.backgroundOwner`, below the window's and never lower, the derived folder's launch look included.
  - The People gallery's read (`PeopleGallery`) shall be the window's own, no heavy load, and shall run while one waits.
  Example: Given loads asked for before the window starts, none runs until the first grid shows the loaded catalog; given one load running, a second waits until it ends; given a search shown, the index's load runs before a People pass asked first; given a waiting load whose task is cancelled, or an admission shut down while a load waits, that load never runs and the next one gets the turn; given the projection's duplicates build and People pass, they never run at once; given a People act while another load runs, the act shows on its face at once and only the People pass it asks for waits its turn; given a People pass running and a duplicates build and the search readying waiting, the first query is read with an encoder its own need loaded, admitted for the user while the pass still runs and the two still wait; given the derived folder's launch look started from an owner's task, it holds its turn at that task's priority; given a description asked for before the first grid with a People pass and readying a search, it runs after the People pass and before the readying; given a memory hold beginning while a People pass runs with a duplicates build and the index waiting, nothing starts while held, the reopen starts the duplicates build alone, then the index and a build asked later, then the People pass, and the raise is kept after a clean run; given a grant raising the pressure, it raises once for one grant, a thermal hold marks nothing and an unread share moves no requirement; given 20 % available at normal or 25 % at warning, a description starts at once and asks for no share, while a People pass at warning waits; given a description held for critical memory, heat or Low Power Mode, it starts in the step that delivers warning; given a People pass granted at 70 % and then a description that fell to 28 % and warned at 31 %, no requirement is set, the next description starts at warning and the People pass starts at 40 %; given a description running, the People view's pass is granted beside it at once, a duplicates build waits, nothing starts while the two run, and only a description yields its turn; given the shell with a request held in flight, the People view's pass runs to its end while the request is under way; given a second raise from a grant at 78 % that fell only to 60 %, the requirement ratchets from 77 % to 79 % and a clean run keeps it; given a People pass granted at 50 % that read 28 % and warned at 30 %, it waits at 53 % unless its view wants it; given the live room, a People pass that warned waits after the level is normal again while the room keeps looking at 76 %, and starts at the look that reads 77 %; given the live kernel, a description starts at 20 % normal and at 25 % warning with no look.; given the background room held at memory warning, a People pass owed to a catalog change waits, and the user's People act grants it at once and it assigns; given nothing waiting, the user's act starts its own pass under the held room while a later change's pass waits again
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/LoadAdmission.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/Admission.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/PhotoDescriptionWriter.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModel.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelImmich.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogProjection.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/PeopleAssignment.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/DuplicatesProjection.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/PhotoSearchIndex.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/ImmichCatalogSync.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/ImmichAccounts.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/BackgroundOwnerSet.swift`

- **ARC-066** Once every background owner has done what it owes, a catalog with nothing new shall cost nothing. [verified]
  - With the derived-folder load, enrichment and its materializer, metadata refresh, render signatures, preview faces, place names, the search index and its first-query preparation, descriptions, Duplicates, People and its gallery, the projection and an account's sync (its mirror pass included) started as the window starts them (`BackgroundOwnerSet`, at the first grid, under the window's one `LoadAdmission`), each owner shall report when it owes nothing and runs nothing, naming the wake it keeps armed (ADR 0032 rule 8).
  - One more ring of every owner's wake path with no input changed shall then announce no store change, open no write transaction, and cost each owner at most two reads returning no row and no row written, in its own units (`OwnerWork`).
  - The window shall log `op=owners.quiescent` with the time from launch when its owners first all owe nothing.
  - The mirror pass shall read each batch first and write only the files whose pair is owed, and an account's sync shall walk its mirrors only when what the walk reads has moved since its last walk that did not fail: its own listing applied a change, or a fixed library's paths, sizes or dates changed (`CatalogStore.libraryNamespaceRevision`); a library file's digest pairs in its own write, so no digest owes a walk (ADR 0054).
  - Enrichment shall ask the Store again of a row on screen it answered owes nothing only after a write names that row or names none (`PriorityAnswers`, ENR-018).
  - A heavy load's release shall grant the next one before anything is measured, and the footprint it logs shall be read on a queue of its own (`LoadAdmission`).
  - EnrichmentCoordinator shall hold a row it cannot hand off now with its claim, and shall ask no background claim again after one found nothing until its inputs move (ENR-009).
  - An owner that cannot make progress on a row now shall hold the fact that ends the wait and read nothing before it arrives: the preview face reader and the description writer walk only for a change of a kind they read or a pacing deadline, the search index reads no backlog page and no named row while its model will not load until its retry, and no reader opens a write for a page it read nothing from.
  - The People gallery shall read only for a request, a change it reads or a People write; a ring that owes none reads nothing.
  - A root's own state (a listing or scan starting or ending, `CatalogChangedRows.none`) shall ask the projection only to show it: no held read, tile detail, Duplicates or People build is read again for it.
  - An account's listing shall release no awaiting rows, since only a card's or camera's rows await an attachment.
  Example: Given a generated reference-shaped catalog and every owner quiescent at one instant, ringing each owner's wake path once leaves them quiescent again with no change, no write, and per owner no row written, at most two reads and no row read; the same at the owner's size on demand; every backoff an owner arms is longer than the run, so no named wake comes due within the round, which the test asserts from the deadlines the owners named; an account pass that listed nothing with no library changed reads no mirror and asks its server nothing, and a file renamed on disk pairs at the next pass, and a paired one unpairs at the next pass and pairs again once the server lists it at its new path; a stalled footprint read holds back no grant.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/OwnerQuiescence.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/BackgroundOwnerSet.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/PeopleAssignment.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/PreviewFaceReader.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/PhotoDescriptionWriter.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/PhotoSearchIndex.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogProjection.swift`, `OtosTests/RunningBackgroundOwners.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStoreMirrors.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/RootRegistry.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/ImmichCatalogSync.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/LoadAdmission.swift`

- **ARC-067** The Store shall never make a window read wait for a background read's connection (ADR 0002). [verified]
  - The catalog file shall have one pool of `CatalogStore.readerCount` readers.
  - A background read shall take one of `CatalogReadAdmission.backgroundReaders` turns before it enters the pool, in the order asked, give it back on the connection's queue as the connection goes back on every exit, and leave the queue when its task is cancelled while it waits; a window read shall enter at once.
  - The caller's handle shall pick the lane (`CatalogStore.windowReads`, `.backgroundReads`), a Store read shall take the handle's `CatalogReadLane`, and a Store read shall reach a connection only through `CatalogStore.reader(for:)` (lint `read_outside_lane`).
  Example: Given every background turn held on its reader, the next background read waits for a turn and a window read is answered; given one held read ends, the waiting read enters; given a waiting background read is cancelled, it throws, leaves the queue and takes no turn, and no turn is left taken; given every background owner runs on a generated owner-size catalog, every window read of five scope changes is answered, none waits for a connection as long as the live store's stall threshold, and each visits rows within its stated multiple of what it returns or its scope holds (answer times are reported, not asserted); every check asserts the same multiples on a small catalog of that shape with his 1,435 named cells, his mirror pairs and import batches, so an import batch's options read fails when it reads every named cell, and a text match is bounded by the candidates its index returns plus the index's own segment directory.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/Admission.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/LoadAdmission.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogReadLanes.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStorePersistence.swift`

- **ARC-068** CatalogWindowViewModel shall apply Projection's window state newest-wins: a relay off the main actor puts each publication in a `NewestValueMailbox`, and the main-actor consumer applies the newest state there is when it runs, never a state after a newer one and never one a newer one replaced before it ran; user acts stay commands on their own lossless streams. [verified]
  - The applied state shall be observed in parts (`CatalogWindowParts`), so applying a publication invalidates only the window surfaces that read a part it changed.
  Example: Given several publications arrive while the main actor is busy, exactly one apply runs and it applies the newest, with the selection and focus on surviving tiles kept; given the relay's stream yields while the main thread is held, the mailbox still receives the state; given a one-tile hide, only the detail column is invalidated (not the window frame, sidebar, inspector or toolbar); given a republication that changed nothing, no surface is invalidated; given a choice, only the toolbar is invalidated.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Support/NewestValueMailbox.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowParts.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModel.swift`, `OtosKit/ContentView.swift`

- **ARC-069** The window shall hold one copy of an order (ADR 0005, "One copy of an order"): when a whole or keyed re-read of a scope's order is equal to the order its slot holds, `CatalogProjection` shall keep the held arrays (IDs, day buckets, coordinates, sort keys), each where it is equal (`CatalogOrderedAssets.holdingArrays(of:)`), and a scope that keeps every row of its order shall collapse the order's own arrays (`CatalogWindowPolicy.assetIDs`), so the collapse, the published window and the slot reference the same IDs. [verified]
  Example: Given a generated reference-shaped catalog visited through 20 scope changes, each followed by a choice changed on a shown row so the order is read again, the projection's held orders, collapses, stacks, change log and window hold at most 2.2 IDs and 10 bytes of ID arrays per distinct asset, and no more IDs than after All Media's first publication plus 5 %.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Models/CatalogQueryModels.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogProjectionWindowStateBuild.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogWindowPolicy.swift`

- **ARC-070** A media asset's in-memory identity shall be its (library root, relative path) pair held once per process (ADR 0005, "An ID is a place in the process's identity table"). [verified]
  - `MediaAssetID` shall be the 4-byte place of the pair in `MediaAssetIdentityTable`; IDs made from equal paths shall be equal wherever and however they were made, IDs of different paths (two Unicode spellings included) shall differ, and an ID shall give back exactly the paths it was made from; the place shall never be persisted or logged.
  - Ordering IDs by path (`relativePathPrecedes`, `pathPrecedes`, `relativePathBytesPrecede`) shall give exactly the order `String`'s `<` (or SQLite's binary collation) gives the paths, decode no string and take the table's lock once per comparison.
  - A pass that only tells libraries apart shall read every ID's root number under one lock acquisition (`MediaAssetID.rootNumbers(of:)`).
  - The catalog shall size the table's index at open from its rows, so making that many IDs never rebuilds it.
  - Every `op=process.memory` sample shall carry the table's pairs, bytes and largest rebuild (ADR 0005, "One identity table for the process").
  Example: Given IDs made from fresh strings with equal bytes, from other roots, from two spellings of a name, and by 8 tasks at once over 5,000 paths, equality, hashing and the paths read back are as stated, and an ID's description names its paths; given every pair of NFC, NFD, reordered-mark, jamo and astral spellings, the table's order is `String`'s and its byte order the UTF-8's; given 5,000 paths sorted by path, by root and path, and by path then root on a table that counts its steps (`countingSteps`), the sort decodes nothing and locks at most once per comparison, and IDs and faces sort in the decoded strings' order; given 5,000 IDs of three roots, their root numbers are read under one lock acquisition, decode nothing, and are equal exactly where the roots are; given 20,000 IDs made after the index was sized, no slot is moved, and unsized, one rebuild moves at least that many; given a screenful of tiles bound and merged by a tile store on a counting table, the table's decodes and lock acquisitions stay within their bounds per tile; the process's table counts nothing.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Models/CatalogModels.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/TileContentStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Support/ProcessResources.swift`

- **ARC-071** When a read or pass builds a library-scale result whose length it learns while it reads, it shall build it without leaving outgrown buffers in malloc. [verified]
  - It shall collect into mapped regions (`MappedArrayBuilder`, mapped through `MappedMemory`, where a failed mapping traps, ADR 0056), which it unmaps, and hand back an array of exactly its count.
  - A grouping shall make each group at its length (`Dictionary(exactlyGrouping:by:)`, lint `grouping_grows_arrays`).
  - A bounded page shall be one buffer reserved at its bound (`Array(page:atMost:)`).
  - A statement a read or write asks once per row or page shall be prepared once per connection.
  Example: Given a build, it gives the elements appended in order, and one dropped unfinished releases them; given an exact grouping, its groups are what grouping gives; on demand and run alone (`OTOS_SCALE_TEST=1`, since its probe hooks every allocation in the process): given the launch and the tour's scope visits on a catalog at 2 % of the owner's shape with every background owner running, no library-scale read (the order and its stacks, the filter options, the duplicates candidates, the People snapshot, a root's rows, the derived folder's walk) grows an array past a 16 KB buffer while it holds a catalog connection; at the owner's size, malloc's dirty bytes less its live bytes after the sequence are at most 64 MB.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Support/MappedArrayBuilder.swift`, `OtosKit/Support/MappedMemory.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogQueryGateway.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStoreDuplicates.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogSQLTypes.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStorePersistence.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/DerivedFileReconciliation.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/PhotoStackModels.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/PeopleModels.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/CatalogFilterOptionTally.swift`, `.swiftlint.yml`

- **ARC-072** Every connection the Store opens on a catalog file, the writer as much as each reader, shall wait on a lock another connection holds on the file (ADR 0002, `ConnectionLockWait`, SQLite's back-off up to GRDB's ten-second reader limit), installed before its first statement, and each wait that begins shall write `op=catalog.lock.wait`. [verified]
  Example: Given another connection holds the catalog file's exclusive lock, as the last connection to a WAL file does while it closes, when the Store's pool opens on it, the writer reports that it waits and opens once the lock is let go, rather than failing the open with `SQLITE_BUSY` at its first pragma.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStorePersistence.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`


## Media And Originals

- **MED-001** Otos shall keep user originals read-only by default. [verified]
  - The sole current filesystem exception shall be explicit user-initiated Move to Trash, which uses recoverable system Trash.
  - No sidecar write shall be permitted.
  - The exception's only byte access shall be the deliberate `MaterializationCoordinator` download of a dataless member that system Trash cannot otherwise accept.
  - Permanent deletion (`FileManager.removeItem`) shall happen only when the user answers `Delete` to an explicit per-occurrence `Delete … immediately?` prompt for items that cannot reach the Trash (APP-027), never automatically.
  Example: Given a feature reads media roots, it does not delete, modify, rewrite, or move originals; given the user explicitly chooses Move to Trash, only catalog-proven members are passed to `FileManager.trashItem`, companions precede primaries, a dataless member is materialized first and is never passed to `trashItem` while dataless, `removeItem` runs only after an explicit `Delete` answer, and partial filesystem truth is retained.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/CatalogScanner.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/EnrichmentCoordinator.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/MediaEnricher.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/MediaTrashCoordinator.swift`
  Not covered by tests: provider-backed dataless materialization and production system-Trash runtime remain beyond the current harness cases

- **MED-002** The app shall not fabricate user-visible or domain facts. [verified]
  Example: Given a date, path, provider state, progress value, count, or capability is unknown, the UI/domain model keeps it unknown or explicitly qualified.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Models/CatalogModels.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogProjection.swift`

- **MED-004** Otos is an image/video product and shall never add audio-specific playback, metadata, thumbnails, icons, classification, or workflows. [verified]
  Example: Given an audio file exists on disk under a library root, it is not cataloged by scan; given future media features are proposed, they do not add audio-specific product behavior.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/MediaEligibility.swift`


## Product Surface

- **APP-001** Otos documentation and user-facing summaries shall claim only behavior proven by current source, tests, and evidence. [verified]
  Example: Given current behavior is documented, it may describe implemented library management, automatic catalog scans, availability, grid browsing, toolbar/menu sort and filter, curation choices, recoverable Move to Trash, background thumbnail and capture-date enrichment, import planning/execution through the full import bar (`APP-029`), and import-batch sidebar history; it does not claim export, search, a metadata inspector, or editing behavior; loupe and compare preview are implemented.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/ContentView.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridView.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/ImportPlanFooter.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelCuration.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelTrash.swift`

- **APP-002** Otos shall provide a SwiftUI sidebar with a shared row layout. [verified]
  - The sidebar shall hold an optional All Media row (only when 2+ fixed libraries exist).
  - Whenever one fixed library exists, the sidebar shall hold an On This Day row under All Media and under it a People row and a Places row, the ways to browse the whole library (APP-069, APP-072, APP-057, ADR 0049, ADR 0047, ADR 0040; People and Places each highlighted too while a person's photos or a place under it are open; owner decision, 2026-11-12).
  - The sidebar shall hold one row for each fixed saved library in the user's own stored order.
  - The sidebar shall hold an Imports section for nonempty import batches (newest first, after Libraries and before Removable), each batch it lists counting the photos its grid shows, a RAW+JPEG pair or a burst imported together once (ADR 0052); only the batches the window shows shall be counted — the newest five, an older selected batch, and every batch while All Imports lists them (`ImportBatchListing`, `docs/research/2026-11-15-import-batch-photo-counts.md`).
  - The sidebar shall hold a separate Removable section for connected removable sources.
  - The sidebar shall hold last a Utilities section holding only the Duplicates row (APP-060), which exists only while there are duplicates and so comes last, where its arrival moves no row.
  - While a removable source's own row is selected and while the batch row of that source's latest run is selected, whatever state the run is in, the import bar shall be drawn for as long as the card is attached; any other import batch shall show that batch and no bar.
  - While the bar is on screen, neither the card's row nor its batch row shall show a status indicator; otherwise exactly one shall show one — the removable row while the card is being prepared, the batch row while its run copies (DES-040).
  - An import shall move the sidebar selection from the card's own row to its batch exactly once, when the sidebar first draws that batch's row (after its first item has landed, even if the run has finished by then), never before the row is drawn, and never from anywhere else.
  - If a removable row disappears while selected, then the selection shall resolve to the ordinary default.
  - View ▸ Show Sidebar / Hide Sidebar (⌃⌘S) shall show and hide the sidebar, the menu equivalent of the toolbar's sidebar button.
  Example: Given two or more fixed libraries exist, the sidebar lists `All Media` and each fixed library by derived folder name, in `library_root.sort_index` order (not alphabetical) — reordering in Settings → Libraries changes this order live; given exactly one fixed library exists and no connected removable/camera source is selected by policy, the sidebar hides `All Media` and auto-selects that library; given nonempty import batches exist, Imports lists localized date/time rows with photo counts and selecting one scopes the grid to that batch; given a batch imported a RAW+JPEG pair, its row counts the pair once, as its grid shows it, and a pair whose files came in two batches counts once in each; given a connected removable volume or MTP camera is present at startup, or attaches later (including a remembered card re-inserted) wherever the user is, the ViewModel selects it; given an attached source only changes availability or activity without a new attach, the selection does not move; given a removable source is connected, it appears under Removable with its raw source display name and kind icon on the shared 20pt icon rail, not in the fixed Libraries section, sorted by display name; given an import is copying and its batch's first item has landed, the selection stays on the card's row until the sidebar draws the batch's row, and moves to that batch then if the user is still on that card's row, even if the run finished meanwhile (and the bar goes with the user to the batch row, which then shows no indicator of its own); given the user is on any other row, including All Imports, when the batch first appears, the selection stays where it is, and returning to the card later is not undone by a second move; given the user leaves the card's row before the batch's row appears, the move is declined then, so a batch whose items leave the catalog before its row is drawn is not waited on, and returning to the card does not pull the user to the batch when its row appears; given the user selects the batch a just-cancelled run produced while the card is still attached, the batch shows no bar and the card's row still does; given the user is standing on a removable row when that source is ejected or pulled after importing, the selection resolves through the ordinary default, not to that source's batch.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Views/SidebarView.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/SidebarSelectionPolicy.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogWindowPolicy.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/ImportBatchFormatting.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModel.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelMediaOperations.swift`, `OtosKit/OtosApp.swift`

- **APP-003** Otos shall provide fixed-library add/remove/reorder only in a dedicated SwiftUI Settings scene with a Libraries tab, removable-source Forget in a separate Removable Media tab, and import policy controls in an Import tab. [verified]
  - Libraries and Removable Media shall be headed tables with their actions beneath (Add Library…, Remove…, Forget…).
  - A library row shall reorder by drag, its Move Up / Move Down menu items and ⌘↑ / ⌘↓, all one intent.
  - Each tab shall size the window to itself (DES-102).
  Example: Given the user opens Settings, they can add one fixed folder at a time, remove a selected fixed library, or reorder libraries by dragging a row, using `Move Up`/`Move Down` in its context menu, or ⌘↑/⌘↓ on the current selection — all three call the one `moveLibrary(_:_:)` intent, which names the library and the move (up, down, or before another library) rather than positions and is applied to the Store's current order, so quick successive moves compose; given remembered removable sources exist, they can Forget a selected source from Removable Media; given the Import tab, they can toggle sidecar import, source deletion, auto-eject, and folder/filename policies without receiving `ViewModel` or plan payloads; given the main window is shown, it does not expose a manual add/remove/reorder flow for fixed libraries.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Views/SettingsView.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelMutation.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`
  Not covered by tests: Settings tab ownership, absence of main-window add/remove/reorder, and the SwiftUI reorder UI itself need behavioral coverage

- **APP-004** Otos shall support multiple saved library roots and reject exact duplicate, ancestor, or descendant root overlaps. [verified]
  Example: Given an existing root is saved, adding the same path, its ancestor, or its descendant fails without changing catalog state.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/CatalogPath.swift`

- **APP-005** Otos shall automatically reconcile saved fixed libraries on launch (replaying their FSEvents history, walking them when it cannot vouch, ARC-025), newly added fixed libraries after add, newly attached removable filesystem volumes immediately, and filesystem-event rescans for the affected watched root while the app is running, with no manual scan button, command, or user-facing scan API. [verified]
  Example: Given the app starts, a library is added, a removable volume attaches, or files change under a watched root while running, scanning begins automatically for the affected root(s); given source views are inspected, no visible manual scan or rescan control exists.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogFilesystemWatcher.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/RemovableVolumeMonitor.swift`

- **APP-006** Otos shall show every visible cataloged image or video asset in a user-adjustable (3–9 columns, default 6, persisted across launches) responsive square grid cell whose visible face is the thumbnail's native aspect-fit rect once enriched, without filename or relative-date text on the tile. [verified]
  - A tile with no thumbnail yet shall show a dim, centered media-kind glyph on the placeholder face — photo (image), r.square (RAW), or film (video) while not yet enriched; photo.slash (image/RAW) or video.slash (video) when enrichment stamped the asset with no obtainable thumbnail — never a spinner or shimmer, and never a lower-right badge on loading or broken tiles.
  - Once a real thumbnail has painted: video tiles shall show clip length center-justified in a lower-right shaded badge (film icon only when duration is missing); raw image tiles shall show the word RAW in the same shaded style; plain images shall show no badge.
  - The badge shall be hidden when the face is under 56 pt.
  - Small/user-visible structural applies, same-scope reorder-only applies (enrichment trickle), thumbnail arrival, the selection ring, and grid zoom shall animate with tuned timing.
  - While the user is actively scrolling, same-scope reorder applies shall suppress the move animation duration (keep the true-diff path) because mid-scroll moves are invisible churn.
  - The reloadData-class fallback for scope-changed reorders and membership applies of 1,000 or more changed items shall stay unanimated.
  Example: Given a library scan finds eligible image and video files, each visible asset has one square grid cell; given the asset is enriched, the visible tile face snaps to the thumbnail's aspect-fit rect with empty window background outside it; given no thumbnail has ever decoded for that asset in the current process, the tile uses a full-square neutral placeholder face with a dim media-kind glyph (including r.square for loading RAW and slash glyphs when enrichment stamped thumbnail-unavailable), no filename/date labels, and no lower-right badge; given a thumbnail paints for the first time, the icon disappears and the image fades in (a cache-hit repaint stays instant); given a painted video with known duration, the lower-right badge shows center-justified clip length; given a painted video without duration, the badge shows a film icon; given a painted raw image, the badge shows RAW; given a painted plain image, there is no lower-right badge; given only tile detail changes, existing visible tiles reconfigure without disappearing; given background enrichment reorders or large-applies surviving visible assets, rendered thumbnails remain painted through the no-blank grid invariant even when AppKit recycles collection items and a surviving tile's selection ring never blinks; given the user zooms via the View menu, toolbar −/+ buttons, or a pinch gesture, the grid reflows to 3–9 equal columns with the viewport-center tile kept anchored, and relaunching preserves the chosen column count; resizing keeps the current column count's columns equal; given a zoom request is superseded (e.g. the toolbar buttons request 6→5→6) while a background structural apply is in flight, the grid's installed column count always converges to the ViewModel's published value and never desyncs, and a zoom swap and a structural apply never run concurrently in either direction. 256 px thumbnails look soft at 3–4 columns on Retina, an accepted limitation.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridView.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridBridge.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridMutationGate.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridStructuralApply.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridZoom.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridZoomPolicy.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/GridColumnCount.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModel.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaAssetTileView.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaAssetTilePresentation.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridAnimation.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridLayout.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/TileContentStore.swift`

- **APP-007** When the user removes a library, Otos shall always require confirmation, delete Otos catalog rows for that root, and leave original media files untouched. [verified]
  - The removal shall hold the root's namespace lease while it runs, so no import, reorganization or Move to Trash can begin on the root mid-removal.
  - A removal asked for while another owner holds the root shall wait for it: the user is told so (DES-091), the library stays cataloged and watched, and the release of that owner's lease hands the root straight to the removal, so nothing else starts on it in between.
  - Forgetting a removable source shall be the same removal, and a waiting removal shall follow a card that remounts at another path.
  - A source attaching to a root shall hold its lease for its few writes: a removal asked for meanwhile waits for it without a notice and then removes the root as it now is, and an attachment that finds a removal holding the root waits for it and attaches as a new source.
  - The wait shall last for this process: a library whose holder never finishes before the app quits stays.
  - If a removal's delete fails, then it shall leave the library cataloged, watched again and rescanned.
  Example: Given the user confirms removal, the library root and media rows disappear from Otos while files remain at their original paths; given an import holds the library, the user is told it will be removed when the import finishes, the library and its rows remain, and when the import releases it the library is removed with no other owner able to take it first.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/SettingsView.swift`

- **APP-008** Otos shall not show catalog failures through a generic user-facing catalog message or alert in the current surface; removable-volume eject failures and explicit Move to Trash failures are the actionable exceptions. [verified]
