- **APP-010** The media grid shall support selection — a single click selects one tile (a plain click on an already-selected tile commits on mouse-up so a drag can carry the selection, APP-038), ⌘-click toggles a tile, ⇧-click extends a contiguous range from the anchor, ⌘⇧-click extends that range while keeping existing selected islands, and ⌘A selects all. [verified]
  - The media grid shall draw an accent ring around the visible thumbnail or placeholder face.
  - The media grid shall preserve scroll and selection across same-scope structural applies.
  - When the sidebar scope changes, the media grid shall reset scroll plus clear selection.
  - The media grid shall keep the user's place across a sort or filter change (APP-042).
  - Mouse selection shall be model-owned: a pure `GridSelection` value (selection, anchor, base) owned by `ViewModel` is the single source of truth and `NSCollectionView.selectionIndexPaths` is a render output the AppKit controller writes from it, never an input read mid-gesture.

Drags and destructive keyboard actions remain absent.

- **APP-011** Media grid mouse selection shall be ephemeral state owned by `ViewModel` and keyed by `MediaAssetID`, held as a pure `GridSelection` model (selection, ⇧-range anchor, base "islands"). [verified]
  - The AppKit collection-view controller shall be a renderer of that model: `NSCollectionView.selectionIndexPaths` is a render output the controller writes from the model as the last operation of each mouse gesture.
  - `MediaGridCollectionView.mouseDown` shall not call `super.mouseDown`, so AppKit's native mouse-selection tracking loop never runs and there is no competing commit; the model shall never be read from `selectionIndexPaths` to decide a gesture.
  - Selection prune/clear for grid identity changes shall run in `ViewModel.applyState` before any AppKit structural apply; apply completion and SwiftUI updates shall only render the model.
  - Live `selectionIndexPaths` shall be reconciled into the model only for gated native keyboard selection (arrows, ⌘A, ⇧-arrows), never for programmatic delta echoes.
  - When a reconcile's live selection is exactly one item (an unmodified arrow move), it shall also move the anchor to that item, so the anchor — which is both the ⇧-range pivot and the active item loupe entry focuses (APP-020) — follows plain keyboard navigation, while a multi-item reconcile (⇧-arrows, ⌘A) shall keep the pivot.
  - The selection shall not be persisted.
  Example: Given a background enrichment or scan re-applies the same descriptor, the selection and its highlight are preserved; given a selected asset disappears, it is dropped via `applyState` prune before the grid renders; given the shift-click anchor survives an order change that removed other identities, its recorded base is pruned to the surviving identities so a later shift-range target equals the live selection; given the anchor itself is removed, anchor and base clear; given the sidebar scope changes while the same identity still exists, selection clears in `applyState` instead of restoring the survivor; given only the filter or sort changes, selection is pruned to the survivors like any other order change (APP-042); given the user clicks a tile and then arrow-navigates to another, the anchor moves with the arrows so Return opens loupe on the arrow-navigated asset.

- **APP-012** When the user invokes ⌘C / Edit ▸ Copy or the context menu's Copy, Otos shall copy the current targets to the system pasteboard as one pasteboard item per photo carrying only that file's `public.file-url`, written at once (ADR 0012). [verified]
  - The file shall be chosen by the hand-off (APP-051): the original for an unedited still that is not a RAW or an untrimmed video, a prepared full-size file otherwise.
  - The targets shall be the Grid selection in display order, the Loupe focus, or only the active Compare pane, the same targets every other Photo command acts on (APP-027), enabling paste into Finder and attachment in Messages, Mail and web pages.
  - Every file shall be on this Mac when it is written: a cloud-only original is downloaded first (APP-051).
  - Every Copy surface shall call the one ViewModel intent `copyCurrentTargets`; the grid shall implement no responder-chain `copy:`.
  - ⌘C shall claim the pasteboard at the command (`MediaGridClipboard.claim`, logged `op=ui.copy phase=claimed`), so a paste while the photos are prepared pastes nothing rather than what was there before.
  - The files shall be written only while that claim is still the pasteboard's (`changeCount`), so a Copy that finishes after another app or a newer Copy wrote it leaves that alone (`phase=superseded`).
  - A newer Copy shall cancel an older one still being prepared and skip one still queued.
  - The command shall be available whenever photos are targeted or text holds the keyboard, and whether it copies text or photos shall be asked of AppKit when it runs (`KeyboardFocus.live`, logged `op=editCommand phase=copy route=`).
  - While text holds the keyboard, in any window, Cut, Copy, Paste and Select All shall act on the text (DES-093).
  - Each write shall log `op=ui.copy phase=written` or, when the pasteboard refuses it, `phase=refused`.
  Example: Given a Grid selection and ⌘C, the pasteboard holds one `public.file-url` item per selected asset in display order and no string; given Compare, it holds only the active pane's asset, and switching the active pane switches what Copy writes; given Loupe, it holds the focused asset; given Finder receives the paste, the files are copied; given ⌘C on a cloud-only photo, the pasteboard is empty until it is downloaded; given a newer Copy while one is prepared, only the newer is written; given another write after the claim, the late Copy writes nothing; given photos targeted, Copy is available whatever the keyboard record says; given no target and no text, Edit ▸ Copy is disabled.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/OtosApp.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridClipboard.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/MediaItemMenuModel.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/CatalogModels.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelSelection.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelCuration.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelHandOff.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/KeyboardFocusRelay.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelInspector.swift`

- **APP-013** The main-window toolbar shall carry, in DES-114's four groups, the view-mode picker and the zoom control; then one native filter popover and one complete-order sort menu; then per-item acts on the current targets; then the Info (APP-041) and Edit toggles; and, while one place under the Places map is open, a leading `Back to Map` item (APP-058). [verified]
  - The view-mode picker shall have no segment selected while Places shows its map, where a segment opens the area the map shows — APP-058; it shall be disabled, never hidden, on the duplicates list, on On This Day — no segment selected there either, APP-070 — and before the map has first reported its viewport.
  - The zoom control shall be the View ▸ Zoom Out / Zoom In intents in every mode (grid density in the grid, the image in the loupe, the camera on the Places map), enabled exactly when those menu items are.
  - The sort menu shall be disabled in place on On This Day, whose order is its own, with the reason as its tooltip — DES-038, APP-069.
  - The per-item acts shall be a Like / Not Rated / Dislike segment showing the one choice every target shares (filled) or none, the Share button every Share path drops from (APP-036), and a Finder-style Action menu holding Open in <Default App>, Open With ▸, Reveal in Finder and Move to Trash.
  - The per-item acts shall each call the intent their menu-bar twin calls and shall be disabled, never hidden, without a target (DES-053); the choice segment shall read no state for more than 64 targets, or when the targets' choices could not be read.
  - View > Show Filter shall open the same popover.
  - The filter shall show available Images/RAW/Videos parent toggles directly above their scoped child types, without a second File Types submenu or redundant All Items row.
  - Parent rows shall select/clear every child and show mixed state for partial selection; no selections shall mean no active filter; Clear Filters shall be disabled when inactive.
  - File types shall carry MediaMetadata's names (ADR 0039): the catalog stores one token per type (`HEIF` for heic/heif/hif, `JPEG` for jpg/jpe, `TIFF` for tif), from the type the bytes settled or else the extension's, so each type is one filter row; a filter restored from an older build shall be renamed the same way.
  - The filter popover shall keep `Filter` and `Clear Filters` in place and scroll its sections as one region no taller than the screen shows under the toolbar (DES-115).
  - A filter shall belong to the sidebar row it was set in: choosing another row, by the user or automatically, starts it unfiltered, while the row's leaves (an area or stack under Places, a person under People, a group under Duplicates) and Back from them keep it.
  - Under the file types, the filter's People section shall list every visible person with a name (favourites first; an unnamed one only while checked) and `Unidentified Faces`, each a checkbox; several checked rows shall be photos with all of them (ADR 0047); a person the filter holds who is hidden or merged away shall leave it.
  - The filter's choices — file types and places — shall be those present in the scope of the shown sidebar row (`SidebarSelection.sidebarRow`): a library offers only what it holds, never what only a card holds, and a leaf is offered its row's choices.
  - Under People, the Places section shall list every country Apple Maps named for the located items in that scope, most items first, each opening onto its towns, most items first (a country with some towns checked is mixed), every place the filter holds that nothing named has keeping a row — a held town under its country after the named towns, a held country after the named countries — and `No places named yet` before any is named.
  - People, the countries and each country's towns shall show their first five rows plus any checked row past them, with `Show All N People` / `Countries` / `Towns` and `Show Less` in place, and the whole people or country list shall show a search field once there are more than eight (`docs/design/people.md`, `docs/design/places.md`).
  - A photo shall match when its map cell is named one of the checked countries or towns in the language the filter holds them in; checking a country shall take all its towns and unchecking one town of a checked country shall keep the rest.
  - Once the catalog holds no names in that language — the Mac's language changed and the old names were forgotten — every checked place shall leave the filter, its saved place and the popover, since nothing the catalog stores names a place apart from its name (ADR 0051).
  - Sort shall expose Date — Newest First/Oldest First, Filename — A to Z/Z to A, Size — Largest First/Smallest First, and Likeness — Least Like Sam First/Most Like Sam First as eight mutually exclusive choices; the likeness orders shall name the person whose photos show and shall be disabled, never hidden, elsewhere, where the reads use the default order.
  - Info and Edit shall be toggles the toolbar draws pressed while their surface is shown (DES-097).
  Example: Given a whole kind is selected, its parent is checked; given only some children are selected, it is mixed; given the checked parent is cleared, all its children clear; given nothing is selected, all items appear and Clear Filters is disabled; given HEIC, HEIF, or HIF exists, one HEIF child matches all; given every available kind is selected, the descriptor normalizes to no active filter; given a sort choice is selected, one native checkmark identifies the complete order; given an active filter has no matches, the grid shows `No Matches` with Clear Filters; given Sam and Ian checked, only photos with both show, and `Unidentified Faces` shows photos with a face of no person; given Sam's photos, Likeness — Least Like Sam First puts the photo least like her first, and elsewhere, a filter on her included, it reads the default order; given the countries and towns named, they are offered most items first; given a held town its country is offered without, and a held country nothing names, each has a row; given a library and a second library with a town and a file type of its own, each library's row offers only its own and All Media and an area under Places offer both; given a place held in English, it stays while English names are held and leaves the filter once they are forgotten, the rest of the filter kept; given eight people and the seventh checked, the first five and the seventh show until Show All; given an unnamed person, the filter does not offer them unless checked.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/ContentView.swift`, `OtosKit/OtosApp.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/CatalogToolbarControls.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/CatalogQueryModels.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/PlaceModels.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridView.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogQueryGateway.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogWindowPolicy.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogProjectionWindowStateBuild.swift`

- **APP-024** Otos shall give each cataloged image or video exactly one durable curation choice: Liked, Not Rated, or Disliked. [verified]
  - New rows shall default to Not Rated.
  - The overlay shall remain independent from selection, scope, media kind, import state, and filesystem facts, shall be preserved by import/reorganization row copies, and shall be purged only with proven row deletion or root removal.
  - Photo menu commands, scoped P/U/X, VoiceOver actions, and tile/pane controls shall all call one ViewModel intent and one Store transaction.
  - The pointer control shall offer two toggle acts, not three targets (DES-066): `MediaChoiceToggle` owns both the outline/filled symbol pair and the single resolution "pressing the act an item already carries returns it to Not Rated", so the AppKit tile and the SwiftUI Loupe/Compare control render one intent rather than re-deriving it.
  - Neutral shall keep its keyboard (`u`), menu, and VoiceOver paths.
  - Curation shall never be color-only: the selected state carries the accessibility value `liked`/`not rated`/`disliked` once on the item — the grid tile itself (`MediaAssetTileView`) or the Loupe/Compare hover region (`MediaChoiceHoverRegion`) — rather than a redundant "Selected"/"Not selected" on each hover button, and Like/Mark as Not Rated/Dislike are all three exposed as VoiceOver custom actions on that same item rather than separate Tab stops (DES-068, DES-085).
  - The hover group shall be laid out from the aspect-fit content rect — the thumbnail face — inset by one margin from its upper-left corner, so it rides the visible image rather than the cell.
  Example: Given a new or migrated row is read, its choice is Not Rated; given a batch is chosen, one SQLite transaction updates only existing rows and a repeated choice is accepted with zero changed rows; given a scan or enrichment writes the same row, its choice remains; given a curation action is submitted from Grid, filmstrip, Loupe, or Compare, the Store receives the typed choice and the surface does not invent a second state; given the act an item already carries is pressed on any surface, the resolved choice is Not Rated; given a portrait or landscape image letterboxed in its cell, the hover group's origin is the content rect's origin plus the margin, so it tracks the visible thumbnail on both axes; given Full Keyboard Access or VoiceOver on any of the four surfaces, the item announces its curation value once and offers the three choices as custom actions, not per-button Tab stops.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Models/CatalogModels.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStorePersistence.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelCuration.swift`, `OtosKit/OtosApp.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaAssetTileView.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaChoiceControl.swift`
  Not covered by tests: live VoiceOver/Full Keyboard Access announcement was not confirmed by direct interactive observation in this agent environment

- **APP-025** The filter popover shall expose a Curation group above Media with Liked, Not Rated, and Disliked choices. [verified]
  - Choices shall be OR within Curation and AND with media kind/format; zero or all three choices shall mean no curation restriction, and Clear Filters shall clear both groups without changing sort.
  - Media shall always list all three kinds (Images, RAW, Videos), and under each the file types present in the scope of the shown sidebar row (APP-013) — a leaf's row's — plus every file type the filter holds, so every value it holds has a ticked row that clears it.

- **APP-026** General Settings shall persist one default-off `Advance after choosing Liked, Not Rated, or Disliked` preference. [verified]
  - While it is on, every choice, including a same-value choice, shall advance without wrapping in the step the choice is shown in (APP-086), never waiting on the write; if a write fails, then Otos shall select its items again with the failure alert.
  Example: Given the setting is off, a choice stays on its item; given it is on, Grid/Loupe/Compare move to the successor at once; given the successor is absent, the browser does not wrap; given the write failing, the chosen item is selected again.

- **APP-027** When the user invokes Photo > Move to Trash, Command-Backspace, the toolbar Action menu, or the context menu item, Otos shall resolve the current Grid selection, Loupe focus, or active Compare pane and act at once, without a confirmation: the act is undoable from Edit ▸ Undo (APP-039), and the HIG reserves alerts for uncommon or irreversible acts. [verified]
  - Plain Delete shall be unbound.
  - The command shall hide the targets at once by recording a durable pending-trash intent on their catalog rows (ARC-058) and hand them to `MediaTrashCoordinator`, the queue owner, which resumes pending items after relaunch.
  - Once hidden, the Grid selection or Loupe focus shall move to the nearest survivor of the last hidden item in the order shown when the command was issued — the next item, or the previous one when the hidden items ended the list — and the Grid shall clear its selection only when nothing survives.
  - For each item it shall move only catalog-proven primary and `.xmp`/`.srt` members with `FileManager.trashItem` off MainActor, companions before primary, holding the root namespace-mutation lease only around the moves and rechecking under it that every member is still local, and reconcile only successful paths.
  - A dataless cloud placeholder cannot leave its File Provider domain (`trashItem` reports `NSFeatureUnsupportedError`), so dataless members shall first be materialized through `MaterializationCoordinator` at `command` priority without the lease.
  - If a download outcome is transient (timeout, provider unavailable, cancellation), then the item shall stay pending and retry with backoff.
  - If the outcome is definitive (`requestFailed`), or a download would leave the volume below the 5 GB `FreeSpaceFloor` (ADR 0027), then Otos shall ask `Delete “name” immediately?` / `Delete N items immediately?` with a destructive `Delete` and a default `Cancel`; same-kind questions shall merge.
  - `Delete` shall remove the files with `FileManager.removeItem`; `Cancel` shall re-show a failed download's items, and at the floor keep them pending until a later pass finds room, without asking again.
  - If a move fails after download, then Otos shall re-show the item, select it, and show the failure alert; in Loupe the re-selected item shall become the focus and its pane decode shall be requested, so the pane never stays on another item's spinner.
  - Every per-path failure shall be logged as `op=mediaTrash.path phase=failed reason=…` with the `ErrorDiagnostics` fields (domain, code, description, path, underlying error, failure reason, and the userInfo keys present).
  - Device/MTP rows shall be ineligible.
  Example: Given a cataloged primary has a paired companion, the companion is moved first and both rows leave the catalog; given a pending item, it is absent from media queries while its file exists; given the primary's move fails, the item reappears and the failure is reported, and in Loupe the pane shows that item's decode; given dataless members, they download at `command` priority while no lease is held; given a timeout, the item stays pending and later moves; given a definitive download failure, the delete question is asked and `Delete` removes the files without a trash call, while `Cancel` re-shows the item; given the floor, the question is asked once and the download starts when room appears; given pending items from an earlier session, a new coordinator trashes them; given a withdrawn request, the item reappears and nothing is moved; given Loupe on the last item, Move to Trash focuses the previous item, and on a middle item the next one; given the Grid selection ends the list, the item before it is selected.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/MediaTrashCoordinator.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/MediaTrashModels.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/TrashPrompts.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogQueryGateway.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelTrash.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/TrashStatusBar.swift`, `OtosKit/OtosApp.swift`, `OtosKit/ContentView.swift`
  Not covered by tests: production system-Trash and provider-backed runtime (probed manually; see docs/research/trash-dataless-placeholders.md)

- **APP-028** Compare shall be positional: entering defaults the active pane to right, pane click activates without changing the pair, pane choice targets that pane, replacement/slide activates the arriving pane, and Swap Compared Photos swaps assets while keeping the active position and each asset's choice. [verified]
  - The active pane's picture shall carry the grid's selection outline and the selected accessibility trait (DES-096); it marks what the commands act on, not a role (APP-021).
  Example: Given left and right peers are swapped while left is active, left remains the active target after the swap and each asset keeps its persisted choice.

- **APP-029** The import status bar (`docs/design/import-bar.md`) shall render one of seven drawn states — Preparing, No destination, Ready (plus nothing-new and empty-card variants), Ready-needs-attention, Working (plus a cancelling variant), Done (plus incomplete and stopped variants), Failed — from a pure mapper that holds every user-facing string, with one fixed height across all states and at most one prominent action — none at all in the Done states whose act zone has nothing left to offer (DES-052, DES-053). [verified]
  - The import act shall be titled `Import` with no count in every state, because Otos imports everything new and the number is L1's primary fact (DES-036).
  - While the import act is unavailable (Preparing, No destination, a Stopped run awaiting its re-plan, or a click the coordinator is already holding until the plan is ready — `importRequested` on the presentation, not a state, so every count, track and choice stays drawn and there is no `Import queued` title), it shall be the same push button disabled and dimmed in place — one `PrimaryAction` case, never static text (DES-053, DES-035).
  - The bar shall render a progress track that reports the progress of the stage the bar is in — determinate preparation progress while preparing, determinate execution progress while working, and no fall back to an indeterminate track once an honest denominator exists (DES-031, DES-032).
  - The bar shall render per-run destination/eject/erase choices inline next to the act they decide (DES-054) and usable from the first Preparing frame with the small linear track drawn above them in the same zone — the choices do not wait for Ready, and no control appears or moves when the plan lands.
  - The bar shall render a `Show them` reveal that selects and scrolls the affected assets in the grid rather than opening a list.
  - The bar shall be drawn on the card's own sidebar row and on the batch row of that card's latest run (`selectedImportPresentation`; `MediaOperationSourcePresentation.runBatchID`, the batch from its run's first landed item until the card's next run starts), so once an import begins and the selection follows it to its batch the bar goes with it — progress and Cancel, then Done or Failed — and on that batch row the bar owns the status, so the batch row shows no indicator of its own (DES-040).
  - Every other row, an older batch of the same card included, shall draw none, and the bar shall leave with the card.
  - The `Show them` reveal shall be offered on the card's row only, since on the batch row the items it names are not in the grid.
  - The bar shall offer no `Show These Items` navigation in any state — not as a primary action and not as a secondary button beside `Eject Card` — because the items it reports as not copied are on the card, not in the batch.
  - Done's incomplete state shall therefore render an empty act zone (DES-053) and keep the `Show them` reveal, which selects those items in the card's grid, as the only reveal.
  - The card's own row shall never dismiss while the card is attached — it re-derives `Everything on this card is imported` with `Eject Card` and Erase from the plan every time the user returns to it.
  - A run's outcome (copied just now, fully clean, stop reason, incomplete items, erase outcome) shall be presented only while the session's destination is still the library that run copied into (`AttachmentCompletion.destination`), so a destination change withholds it rather than pairing its count with another library's name, and picking the original library again restores it.
  - A figure the bar cannot know shall not be stated: the new items' weight appears only when every size is known, and a floor stop names the room left only when the volume reported it.
  Example: Given a card attaches, the bar shows Preparing then Ready with an honest byte/item count; given items are blocked, the badge names the real cause and disables Erase with a tooltip rather than prose; given execution runs, items/bytes/an omitted-until-meaningful remaining-time estimate advance and Cancel shows a distinct "Stopping…" state while winding down; given some items fail or are skipped as foreign, Done reports "N of M items copied" with a "Show them" reveal instead of the clean checkmark wording, and no button offering to show the batch; given an in-flight, just-cancelled, failed or incomplete run whose card is attached, selecting its import batch shows the card's bar, the same one the card's row shows, without the `Show them` reveal, while an older batch of the same card shows none; given a settled import's card is ejected or pulled, whatever the outcome, no presentation for it remains; given an erase-only run is confirmed, the alert states the "already in library" sentence only when the plan proves it; given every attempted copy entry fails `sourceStillMatches` for the same non-per-file reason (the source itself wasn't ready, not per-file damage), the executor classifies the run as `.failed(reason: .sourceNotReady)` rather than a misleadingly clean-shaped `.completed(failedCount:)`, and Failed names the honest cause ("The camera is still reading its card" / "The card is still being read") rather than falling through to Done; given a `publish()` fires for any reason during execution — not only the executor's own progress callback — the presentation still reports the live in-flight progress rather than resetting to a zero-progress placeholder, because the coordinator's session, not a transient per-call override, is the one stored fact `presentation(for:)` always derives from; given a per-source session preference (e.g. delete-after-import) is toggled while other presentation frames keep arriving, the toggle is reflected once the coordinator publishes it and is never reverted by a later frame, because the ViewModel reads the live preference off the presentation itself rather than mirroring it into a second, independently-refreshed cache; given a byte count is genuinely zero or a destination's display name is not yet resolved, `ImportBarByteFormat.string(bytes:)` renders `0 KB` (never a `max(1, …)`-inflated `1 KB`) and every destination-name clause substitutes the honest generic "the library" (never a lying `""`) — enforced by `script/guard-design-system.sh`'s check that no user-facing string in `ImportBarPresentation.swift` is built from a `?? 0` / `?? ""` default; every one of the `primaryAction` cases honors `drawn.primaryEnabled` through one owner (`ImportPlanFooter.RenderedPrimary`, a pure render-intent value the primary button consumes for title/enabled/default-shortcut/destructive-tint) rather than each case independently re-deriving `.disabled`, closing the gap that let a disabled cancelling-state button render live; the bar's three zones lay out `auto 1fr auto` (*what*/*the act* size to content, *choices* absorbs the slack, DES rule per `docs/design/import-bar.md`), and draw at most one hairline, before the middle zone, only when it is `.choices` or `.progress` — never before the act, and never when the middle zone is `.hidden`; the erase checkbox's disabled-reason tooltip is attached only when a reason exists, and to a wrapping container rather than the disabled control itself, so DES-038's explanation reliably reaches the user; state F-incomplete's "Show them" reveal selects the source card's identity of a failed or skipped-foreign entry — the card's grid is where the bar is drawn, and the item never landed in the destination; a presentation's `runBatchID` names the batch of the attachment's latest run once that batch has at least one durably-published asset (`store.importBatchAssetCount(batch) > 0`), is kept through the run's settlement, the re-plan and `Try Again`, and is cleared when the next run starts; a second refused run leaves the footer mounted on the card's row; given the destination is changed after a run, `copiedJustNow` and `isFullyClean` are absent until the original library is picked again; given a run stops before attempting every planned entry (the user's Cancel; the disk floor per ADR 0027), the executor settles it as `.stopped(reason:…)` rather than as a completion, the coordinator records the reason and the run's own planned count beside the copied count and drops the now-false pre-run plan, and the bar draws state F's Stopped variant from those run facts on the card's row — `Stopped — 12 of 42 items copied`, the cause at L3 (`They’re in <library>` after a Cancel, omitted when nothing was copied), choices present, and the re-plan's remainder as the act (`Import`, disabled until the re-plan has counted it; the card's own act when nothing is left); the stop is stated until the next run starts, a new run's Working or Failed outranks it, and a stopped run is never fully clean so it neither auto-dismisses, auto-ejects nor erases; given the destination volume cannot take the whole card, Import still starts (ADR 0027): the executor admits each entry only while `available − entryBytes ≥ FreeSpaceFloor.bytes` (5 GB), where `available` is the run's `FreeSpaceLedger` view: the lesser of `volumeAvailableCapacityForImportantUsage` read fresh per entry and the run's first reading less every entry it has started or landed, so a copy the figure already shows is not counted twice and a figure that never moves still counts the entries that landed — `entryBytes` being the sizes the plan knows, a file of unknown size admitted on the known bytes with `ENOSPC` as the backstop and logged `phase=admitUnknownSize`, never counted as zero — stops submitting at the first entry that would breach the floor while entries already in flight land, and settles as `.stopped(reason: .diskFloor(bytesFree:))` — a write that refuses for space (`ENOSPC`) stops the run the same way — a card's `copyfile` returning `ENOSPC`, or a camera's staging download whose typed error chain carries `NSFileWriteOutOfSpaceError` or POSIX `ENOSPC` (`CameraByteFailure.outOfSpace`; an untyped error is never classified by its wording) — and that entry is neither counted as failed nor revealed, since it never landed and the re-plan offers it again — so the bar reads `Stopped — 412 of 680 items copied` · `Not enough room on the disk · 5.4 GB left` with the re-plan's `Import` for the remainder; an unknown capacity admits the entry, logged once per run `phase=capacityUnknown`; the end-of-run erase sweep never runs after a floor stop while delete-after-import of entries that landed is unchanged (each erased on its own settle, once proven in the library) and the refused entries are never touched; there is no run-level free-space check and no `The disk is full` failure; given a run fails (the card pulled, the destination lost, the source not ready), the failure is recorded on the session beside the completion — reason, count landed, and the run's batch — rather than held as a phase, so the rebuild the coordinator schedules after every settlement replaces the phase and finds a Ready plan without erasing state G: the presentation stays `.failed` until a new attempt starts or the user presses `Try Again`, which clears the recorded failure and reveals the re-plan; planning failures (no destination, no snapshot, a builder error, an unreadable destination past its retry budget) are the distinct state `.planningFailed(reason:)` — a phase that a successful rebuild replaces — and the bar draws them as state G's planning variant, `Couldn’t prepare the import` with the same cause clause, choices and `Try Again` as a run failure, never `Couldn’t finish the import` for a run that never started; given the plan build finds a destination folder that exists but cannot be listed, the build is deferred rather than published — the phase returns to inventory, the last proven plan keeps presenting, and a bounded backoff (1 s, 4 s, 16 s, 60 s) re-plans, each attempt logged as `op=import.plan phase=deferred reason=destinationUnreadable attempt=… folders=…` and the enumeration failure itself as `op=plan.namespace.enumerate phase=unreadable folder=… error=…` — so a plan whose entries are blocked as "couldn't check" is never presented; past the last delay the condition is the planning failure `destinationUnavailable` (state G), which `Try Again` and any planning wake clear by rebuilding; given a run completes, the plan it executed from is dropped and the settlement itself presents (Done) until the re-plan lands, so no stale pre-run Ready is drawn between two Done frames; given every item in a plan is blocked, state D offers no act rather than an enabled `Import 0 Items` (DES-053), and a suffix-exhausted item names its cause as `Otos couldn’t find a free name for N items in <library>`; given a Ready plan's bytes plus the 5 GB floor exceed the destination volume's available capacity as sampled by the coordinator on that Ready publish (`MediaOperationSourcePresentation.destinationAvailableBytes`, read with the executor's own `importantUsageCapacity` and only for states that carry a plan), state C's L3 reads `Not everything will fit yet · <available> available` in place of the already-in-library clause, the act stays `Import` for the whole plan, and nothing is gated — the line is two measured values and Otos's stopping rule, not an estimate; it is absent when everything fits or the volume did not answer.

  Not covered by tests: the erase-tooltip fix and the light/dark, no-mouse, Reduce-Motion visual passes (DES-022, DES-029, DES-080, DES-075) were not confirmed by direct interactive observation in this agent environment — see the DoD report

- **APP-014** The ViewModel's query descriptor shall own browser sort and filter state, and Otos shall persist that state only as part of the browser place saved at quit (APP-043). [verified]
  Example: Given no stored browser place, the ViewModel's descriptor starts at All Media, no active filter, and Date — Newest First; given the user changes filter or sort, only the current process state changes until quit, and no database row or other preference records it; given an older projected state arrives after a newer local sort/filter intent, including a rapid change that toggles back to the previously displayed descriptor, the ViewModel updates query controls only when the projected state's query generation exactly matches the newest local intent generation, and the next query intent is built from the newer local descriptor.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModel.swift`

- **APP-015** The media grid shall never replace a painted same-asset thumbnail with placeholder content in the current process; placeholder content shall be permitted only before that asset has ever decoded a thumbnail in the process. [verified]
  Example: Given a reorder-only structural apply while visible thumbnails are painted, zero visible surviving tiles render placeholder content; given a tile-detail query fails or returns no row for a visible painted asset, the painted thumbnail stays; given a new thumbnail key fails to decode for the same asset, the previous painted thumbnail stays; given the user scrolls back to a previously displayed asset still held in tile store (pinned, or among the off-screen tiles it keeps: the sixteen screens of the visible set's size used most recently, within the byte budget), the thumbnail paints synchronously with no placeholder frame; given a backlog of reorder publications and scrolling, the store holds no more than the visible tiles and its off-screen limit; given the system appearance changes between light and dark while thumbnails are painted, every painted tile keeps its thumbnail (the tile's thumbnail layer is owned by Otos and has no AppKit drawing to repaint over it); the no-blank rule is structural in tile store (a pinned/visible painted image never regresses to nil), so no runtime paint watchdog is required.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Views/TileContentStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaAssetTileView.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridBridge.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridPrefetch.swift`

- **APP-016** All Media and Places shall include fixed-library roots only; removable volumes and MTP cameras shall be visible only through their own source rows. [verified]
  Example: Given a fixed library and a removable source both contain cataloged media, All Media shows only the fixed-library assets; given the removable source row is selected, the grid shows that source's media; given the source is unavailable, its rows are hidden from visible queries but cataloged overlay remains.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/CatalogProjection.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`

- **APP-017** Otos shall attach removable filesystem volumes as removable sources, scan them immediately at user-initiated priority (the user watches the card while its import bar checks it; a fixed library's walk stays utility, `ScanLane.taskPriority`), remap them by stable source identity on reattach, show eject controls only when ejectable, preserve rows/overlay while detached, and support explicit Forget. [verified]
  - A mounted disk image shall never qualify as a removable source, regardless of `volumeIsRemovable`.
  - When a card's mount completes, Otos shall attach the card, even when DiskArbitration reported its volume path before the mounted-volume listing held it (a freshly inserted card mounted through FSKit).
  - A completed mount shall ask for the mounted set to be read again, and a removable volume the read leaves out shall be logged with the fact that excluded it (`op=removableVolume.rejected`).
  - A card's scan shall run in its own lane (`ScanLeaseController.ScanLane`), never queued behind a fixed library's scan.
  - While an attached card or camera is being read, the rows it retained from its last attachment shall stay out of every user-facing read, import planning and enrichment claims until this attachment's scan or enumeration observes each file (`media_asset.awaiting_presence`, ADR 0013).
  - The grid shall fill as the source is read and never show files it held last time.
  - The completed read shall delete what it never saw.
  - Detach, launch and a read that fails or ends incomplete shall release what the read never reached, so the source shows what it last held rather than a partial or empty card.
  - When a card is resumed after a refused eject, Otos shall read it again.
  - While a card is scanned and nothing on it has been seen yet, its grid shall show **Reading Card** (`CatalogMediaEmptyState.cardReading`, with progress, DES-003 title role) rather than the no-media state; its first tile shall replace it.
  Example: Given a removable volume attaches with identity `volume:<uuid>`, Store records or updates that source and queues a front scan; given the same source returns at a different path, the source row path is remapped instead of duplicated; given two mounted volumes report the same UUID, runtime identity is disambiguated by mount path rather than trapping; given a watched removable source is already attached when filesystem watching starts, it is included in the watched-root set; given eject is requested, the sidebar row stays visible with a spinner while Store quiesces work without marking the source unavailable — awaiting its cancelled scan and offering no new enrichment claims there until resumed, when the source claimable again is announced (`.detail`) so a camera, which no scan follows, is read again — Otos's own readers on the card are released (APP-008), and an off-MainActor DiskArbitration unmount-and-eject runs; given unmount succeeds, the row disappears only after the real detach/unmount event; given unmount fails busy, the row stays visible and the main window shows the in-use alert; given another refusal, the row stays visible with an alert naming the BSD error the refusal carries, or no cause when it carries none; given Otos's own unmount fails, the source resumes at once; given an unmount asked for elsewhere announces itself (DiskArbitration's unmount approval) and never completes, which the system does not report, the source resumes after `RemovableVolumeMonitor.unreportedUnmountRefusalLimit`; given DiskArbitration reports an unmount request and then the volume's removal, the source is quiesced and then detached in that order, because every removable report arrives in one ordered stream; given a second unmount request quiesces a source again, the first request's limit never resumes it; given it detaches, the source becomes unavailable and enrichment claims are released without deleting rows; given the user chooses Forget, the source row and overlay are removed; given scanner sees hidden, AppleDouble, Spotlight/trash, `AVF_INFO`, or `M4ROOT/SUB` paths, those files are not cataloged; given a volume reports `volumeIsRemovable == true` but its live `DiskArbitration` device model is the `DiskImages.framework` sentinel `"Disk Image"` (any `hdiutil`-mounted `.dmg`, app installers included), it is never offered as a removable source. Given a mount completes after the last read of the mounted set, the monitor reads it again. Given a library and a card attached, the card's walk runs at user-initiated priority and the library's at utility.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/RemovableVolumeMonitor.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStorePersistence.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/ScanLeaseController.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogQueryGateway.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogSchema.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogScanner.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogWindowPolicy.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/CatalogMediaEmptyStatePresentation.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/MediaEligibility.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/SidebarView.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/SettingsView.swift`

- **APP-018** Otos shall attach MTP/PTP cameras as removable camera sources through ImageCapture, reject mass-storage devices, show catalog rows and provisional thumbnails from device facts, route materialization by root kind rather than by matching a file path, and stage bytes for enrichment and keep them for the life of the app session. [verified]
  - An item shall be cataloged at its parent folders and the name the device lists — `ICCameraItem.name`, unique in its folder, never `originalFilename`, which an iPhone repeats as `FullSizeRender.heic` for every edited photo.
  - If the camera reports no name for an item, or two items claim a path, then the item has no path that would survive the next enumeration, so Otos shall skip and log it rather than give it an invented one.
  - An eject shall keep the staged bytes, because they are already off the camera and discarding them would re-download the whole device on the next attach.
  - Staged bytes shall be cleared at exactly three points, each covering a case the others cannot.
  - Staged bytes shall be cleared **per item the moment its copy settles durably in the library** (a failed or blocked entry keeps its bytes so a retry does not re-download them, and crash recovery releases the same way).
  - Staged bytes shall be cleared **at quit**, in an unbounded window with a visible "Clearing cached camera files…" state, because `applicationWillTerminate` is bounded and macOS ends the process partway through — which is why tens of gigabytes survived a clean quit while a small test case did not.
  - Staged bytes shall be cleared **at launch** as the crash-safe backstop, which must run before camera monitoring starts and off the main thread.
  - Staging trees shall be per process instance, so the launch sweep removes what an ended session left without touching a second running instance's in-flight transfer.
  - A release path shall be proven to lie inside this instance's staging tree before anything is deleted; one outside it shall be refused and logged, never removed.
  - MTP/PTP cameras shall show a dismissable connection guidance banner when selected.
  - Every ImageCaptureCore callback shall claim no isolation the framework does not give — delegate methods `nonisolated` on a plain (never `@preconcurrency`) conformance, and every completion/result closure passed to a `request…` API written so it inherits none — each hopping explicitly to its main-actor owner.
  - ImageCaptureCore delivers some callbacks — `cameraDevice(_:didReceivePTPEvent:)` among them — on its own `ICDeviceOperationQueue`, and claiming isolation turns that into a runtime executor check that traps rather than a hop.
  - The rule shall cover closures because it was first written for delegate methods only, and `requestDownload`'s completion then trapped the app the moment device staging began working (`_dispatch_assert_queue_fail` under `swift_task_checkIsolatedSwift`); `requestThumbnailData` and `requestDeleteFiles` carried the same latent claim.
  - Owner state written by those hops shall be sticky or monotonic, so hop order cannot change an observed outcome.
  - While a camera is connected, its catalog shall follow it: every add, remove or rename the camera reports after its catalog is complete runs one more pass (newest-wins, passes never overlap) that records the list, deletes rows only when the list held still, and stages and thumbnails only items no earlier pass saw.
  - The ranged-read probe shall run once per attach.
  Example: Given an ImageCapture camera reports no mounted volume or mass-storage transport, Otos attaches it as `kind = deviceMTP`; given the device catalog is still enumerating and the camera source is selected, the grid shows a camera-specific reading state; given the device provides rows before catalog stability is proven, Store may make observed rows visible but must not reconcile-delete unobserved rows until the current Store-issued enumeration lease completes with a stable snapshot; given the device provides a provisional thumbnail, Store fills a missing thumbnail path without replacing a final thumbnail; given enrichment needs bytes, the request is routed by the asset's root kind — a `deviceMTP` root is served only by the active camera session, never by the cloud provider — and a device root with no live session answers `deviceUnavailable` rather than deferring to a provider that cannot serve it; given the camera detaches, detach uses the identity recorded at attach time, staging bytes are retained for reuse, and stable source rows become unavailable; given a camera source is selected, the banner recommends direct card insertion or Mass Storage mode for faster browsing and can be dismissed; given ImageCaptureCore delivers a delegate callback on a non-main thread, the app applies it through a main-actor hop instead of trapping.; given a connected camera reports a new shot, the next pass records, stages and thumbnails only that item; given it reports a deletion, the row is removed when the list holds still and kept when it does not; given a camera returns, the rows it held last time show only as its new enumeration reports each file (APP-017)
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/CameraSourceMonitor.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/DeviceStagingRegistry.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridView.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CameraCatalogSync.swift`

- **APP-019** The main window toolbar and View menu shall expose one Grid / Loupe / Compare view-mode switcher (segmented control + ⌘1 / ⌘2 / ⌘3). [verified]
  - All surfaces shall call the same ViewModel `setViewMode` / `openLoupe` / `enterCompare` / `exitToGrid` intents.
  - Mode state shall default to Grid and be restored on relaunch with the browser place (APP-043).
  - Selection shall persist across view-mode switches (grid ↔ loupe ↔ compare round trip preserves the model selection).
  - While the Places scope shows its map, the switcher and ⌘1 / ⌘2 / ⌘3 shall be disabled, `setViewMode` shall refuse, and a loupe or compare left open elsewhere shall exit to grid on entry (APP-057).
  - The same shall hold on On This Day, whose cards and story are its one presentation (APP-070).
  Example: Given the user chooses Loupe from the toolbar or ⌘2, the detail surface shows loupe; given ⌘3, compare opens; given ⌘1 or Esc in loupe/compare, the surface returns to grid with selection intact; given a selected set before entering loupe or compare, the same identities remain selected after the mode round trip and after a freshly created strip/grid controller's first structural apply.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Views/CatalogToolbarControls.swift`, `OtosKit/OtosApp.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelViewMode.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelSelection.swift`, `OtosKit/ContentView.swift`

- **APP-020** Loupe shall show the focused asset aspect-fit in the detail area with a filmstrip of the current scope below. [verified]
  - Entry shall be double-click on a grid tile, Return in the grid, or ⌘2/segment (anchor, else first selected, else first item).
  - The 256 px thumbnail shall paint immediately; the full-size decode shall replace it when ready and never blank a painted preview or show an image belonging to a different asset.
  - The pane's backdrop shall be the `content` surface in every state (DES-022): the zoom view hosting the full decode paints no background of its own, so the swap changes only the image.
  - The video player shall be sized to the video's displayed aspect ratio (track `naturalSize` through `preferredTransform`, measured when the player is prepared) so AVKit has no letterbox to paint black; a video whose size cannot be read shall get the full pane.
  - A strip click shall change the loupe image (focus + selection follow).
  - Every focus change — including a failed Move to Trash re-selecting its item and a reorganization rename remapping the focused identity — shall reconcile the pane, so it never waits on another asset's decode.
  - The focused asset shall be selected and centered in the strip as soon as the strip/grid is window-hosted and laid out — centering must not wait on full-size decode or a later enrichment apply.
  - ← / → shall move focus (selection follows as a plain click) and work immediately after mode entry without a priming click.
  - Esc or ⌘1 shall return to grid — at any zoom, Esc first returns the image to fit (APP-040); double-click on the image shall toggle fit and 100 percent rather than leaving the loupe.
  - When a video is opened in loupe, Otos shall prepare it for playback with no click (DES-087): as soon as focus resolves the asset's `bestKind` to `video`, one owned preparation task applies the same live `SF_DATALESS` gate and interactive `MaterializationCoordinator` demand that images use and builds a paused inline `AVPlayerView` (`controlsStyle=.inline`), so the user starts playback from native controls.
  - Until then the poster shall be the thumbnail.
  - The loupe's video shall have one owner, `loupeVideo` — `.preparing`, `.ready` or `.unavailable` with its reason — set to `.preparing` in the frame preparation starts, so the pane shows one indeterminate indicator centered on it while the bytes or the player are coming (DES-086).
  - The pane-preview request shall own `downloadingSlots` and the video path shall never write it, and the pane shall draw one indicator for either wait.
  - If a preparation finds no player, then the pane shall show, in the indicator's place, `Can’t play this video` with the reason the source or this Mac gave and `Try Again`, which asks by the same path and shows its wait in the frame it is pressed (DES-120).
  - If a Live Photo's motion cannot come, then it shall be shown the same way over its still.
  - Playback shall pause when focus changes, mode leaves loupe, or the pane is torn down.
  - Compare shall stay poster-only.
  Example: Given a double-click on a tile, loupe opens on that asset and the strip centers it in the same interaction before the full-size decode lands; given Esc back to grid, the grid centers the focused asset immediately; given a strip click on another tile, the big image follows that asset (thumbnail immediately, full decode when ready); given a dataless video opened in loupe, materialization is requested at interactive priority without a click and the pane shows the thumbnail plus a centered progress indicator; given a video whose server does not answer, the pane shows `Can’t play this video` with the reason and `Try Again`, and pressing it shows the indicator in the same frame and asks again; given preparation completes while the asset is still focused, inline video controls appear paused; given focus moves before the bytes arrive, the superseded preparation is cancelled and its player is never published; given ← in loupe during video playback, focus advances to the previous display-order neighbor, the previous player pauses and is torn down, and no other asset's frame is shown; given loupe entry, arrows work without a priming click; given compare shows the same video, it never plays, only the poster.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Views/LoupeView.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/LoupeZoomView.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/CompareView.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/LoupeVideoPlayerView.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/PanePresentation.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/VideoPreviewLoader.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelViewMode.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelSelection.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/BrowserViewMode.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/PanePreview.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridCollectionView.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridBridge.swift`

- **APP-021** Compare shall show two peer aspect-fit panes plus a filmstrip with zero role chrome (no Select/Candidate labels, swap, or promote). [verified]
  - The compared pair *is* the selection capped at two; panes shall be positional slots (`left` / `right`).
  - A plain filmstrip click (and ⇧) shall replace the **right** pane; a ⌘-click (and ⌘⇧) shall replace the **left** pane.
  - When the user clicks an asset that already occupies the other slot, compare shall swap instead of duplicating.
  - ← / → shall slide the window `(A,B)→(B,next(B))` / `(prev(A),A)`.
  - Entry shall take the first two selected in display order as (left, right), or one selected + next neighbor as right, or the first two in scope.
  - Each pane shall be identity-keyed (`PanePreview`) and never show another asset's image.
  - Strip focus shall center the most recently replaced slot (entry defaults to right).
  Example: Given two selected assets and ⌘3, both appear side by side; given a plain strip click on a third asset, the right pane is replaced and the left pane keeps its matching preview; given ⌘-click on another asset, the left pane is replaced; given →, the window advances to the next sequential pair.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Models/ComparePairPolicy.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/PanePreview.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/CompareView.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/PanePresentation.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelViewMode.swift`

- **APP-022** Loupe and compare shall show a filmstrip of the full current scope in display order — horizontal scroll, same square tiles, same selection gestures and no-blank paint as the grid — by parameterizing the existing AppKit collection bridge (`.grid` / `.strip`), not a second thumbnail engine. [verified]
  - The strip shall use collection-view horizontal scrolling (not an orthogonal embedded scroller) so scroll-driven detail queries and preheat fire while scrolling.
  - Tile edge shall be user-resizable by dragging the strip's top edge (64–192 pt, default 72, persisted as `mediaStripTileEdge`); drag up enlarges.
  - The 64 pt floor is set by the hover curation group, not by thumbnail legibility: two 28 pt acts plus spacing and the corner margin need 61 pt, so a smaller tile could only show them by shrinking the targets where they are hardest to hit or by overflowing onto the neighbouring tile (DES-067).
  - The handle shall be VoiceOver adjustable with increment/decrement clamped to the same bounds.
  Example: Given loupe is open, the strip shows every asset in the current ordered scope; given a strip click, selection updates through the same ViewModel-owned GridSelection model as the grid; given the user scrolls the strip, visible-detail queries run and thumbnails load for newly visible tiles; given the user drags the strip top edge upward, tile edge grows within 64–192 and survives relaunch; given the smallest tile edge, the whole curation group fits inside the tile at full-size targets.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridPresentation.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridBridge.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/LoupeView.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/CompareView.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModel.swift`

- **APP-023** Full-size loupe/compare decode shall live in `PreviewImageLoader`, which asks the shared materialization owner to make the original local at interactive priority, rechecks before reading, coalesces decode work, and lets the ViewModel reject stale results by expected identity. [verified]
  - A dataless placeholder, or a camera original not yet staged, shall be fetched; a missing in-place original shall not.
  - Interactive materialization of a folder's file shall be one coordinated read (`FileMaterialization.coordinatedRead`, on a thread of its own) — no request schedule, no fail-fast probe, no deadline — which downloads the file before its accessor runs or fails with the provider's error, and which the last waiter withdrawing stops waiting for at once, because the filesystem exposes no signal distinguishing a slow provider from a stalled one (hydration is atomic; `research 2026-09-01`) and only a coordinated read ends with the provider's own answer (`research 2026-11-26`).
  - The pane shall not re-request on a non-image outcome, and its spinner shall track an outstanding request rather than a failed attempt.
  - If a pane's tile-detail read fails, then the pane shall decode and publish nothing — a decode without the row would drop the photo's edit and curation — clear its spinner, and be asked again: a screen-sized request by the next reconcile, a full-resolution one by the next zoom report (ARC-059).
  - Pane thumbnail fallback shall read tile store and, on a miss, decode off the MainActor; decoded pane images shall be owned by tile store, not by the view.
  - Interactive demand shall prevent new device/background dispatch until no interactive request is pending or active; a same-ID lower-priority request shall be promoted before dispatch or joined in flight without duplication.
  - The loader shall hold only what a pane can return to: the screen-sized decodes of the last three photos asked for (both Compare panes and the photo just left), never a full-resolution decode, which only the pane showing it holds, and nothing once the full-size view is left.
  - A screen-sized request shall decode the stored preview at the requested long edge into the form a pane displays without a second copy, and a decode whose every waiter has moved on shall stop before it reads a byte and not be held.
  Example: Given a dataless loupe asset, interactive work dispatches before queued lower-priority work; given a camera original never staged opened at full resolution, it is staged through the device executor and decoded; given the same asset is already requested, preview shares the physical transfer; given focus moves, stale decode completion does not paint; given an interactive download, it is one coordinated read with no request or probe, and ends materialized, cancelled or refused as the read answers; given the last interactive waiter withdraws, the transfer ends and admission accounting returns to zero; given a pane request resolves without an image, the spinner clears and no re-request follows; given the pane's tile-detail read fails, no preview is published, the spinner clears, and the next reconcile publishes the preview; given a full-resolution request's read fails, nothing is decoded, the screen-sized preview stays, and the next zoom report asks again; given four photos opened in turn, a step back to each of the last three paints the decode already made and the first is decoded again; given a full-resolution decode, asking for it again decodes it again while the photo's screen-sized decode stays held; given ten stored previews walked in the full-size view, the footprint holds at most the shown photo and three held previews; given the full-size view is left, no photo stays held and the next visit decodes anew; given a screen-sized request smaller than the stored preview, the decode is the requested size; given a decode lands after the view was left, it is not held; given a decode's only waiter is cancelled, it stops before reading and is not held, while a shared decode keeps running for a waiter still waiting; given the tour's photo steps in the main window (open, next ×3, previous ×3, Compare), the footprint's peak stays within 100 MB of the footprint before and returns to within 20 MB of it once the view is left, with no photo held by the loader.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/PreviewImageLoader.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/MaterializationCoordinator.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModelViewMode.swift`, `OtosKit/ViewModels/CatalogWindowViewModel.swift`

- **APP-030** While the sort is Date (`.bestDate` field, either direction), the grid shall group into day sections; under `.name`/`.size` sort the grid shall render one ungrouped section with neither header nor bar, since a non-date-ordered list has no meaningful day grouping (DES-069). [verified]
  - Each day section shall be headed by a `.headline` date (`RelativeDateText`: Today/Yesterday/Last `<weekday>`/absolute date) and a `.subheadline` item count that scrolls with its run.
  - An app-owned day bar at the top of the grid shall carry a copy of that label for whichever day owns the viewport's top edge, so a date is on screen at all times.
  - The two shall be one label drawn twice — a single view, identical treatment, and the same rect, the header spanning the section's full width rather than its inset content width so the label never shifts between them.
  - Every drawn day label shall carry a DES-023 hairline at its bottom edge — a day separator under an in-flow header, and under the bar the contextual scroll edge macOS draws while content passes beneath a floating element, present only once the grid has left its top.
  - The blank first header shall draw neither label nor rule, and the handoff shall not animate because nothing moves.
  - Every day section shall draw its own in-flow header except the first, whose rect the bar already covers and which rubber-band scrolling past the top would otherwise slide out from under it as a duplicate; its space shall still be reserved.
  - What a header draws shall be a function of the section it is in now and the days last applied, re-derived for every header on screen after each structural apply as well as when AppKit makes one, because a diffable apply keeps the header view of every section whose day survives however far it moves.
  - The bar shall reserve no space and be shown continuously under Date sort — including at the top of the grid, where it rests exactly on the first header and draws the same label in the same rect, so leaving it on costs nothing visible.
  - The bar shall be hidden only where there is no day to name.
  - The bar shall be resolved on every scroll-bounds change rather than behind the scroll-work coalesce, which is a trailing-edge debounce that does not fire until scrolling stops.
  - Where the viewport's top edge sits in the inset gap above a header, the bar shall name the preceding day, as a pinned header does.
  - The grid shall deliberately not use AppKit's `pinToVisibleBounds`: it relocates every header of a section into a container repositioned from the main thread on each bounds change while tiles move on the compositor, and on macOS 26+ attaches an undeclinable scroll-edge blur of the passing tiles (`docs/research/2026-09-04-grid-day-header-pinning-and-scroll-edge-effect.md`).
  - The bar's frame shall be constant, so it cannot separate from the content it labels.
  - Assets with no known capture date shall still get their own section (`nil` bucket) rather than being dropped from the day structure or folded into the ungrouped case.
  - Grouping shall be identical for a library, All Media, and a removable source.
  - The vertical scroller shall start below the day bar, and while its knob is dragged a label beside the knob shall name the bar's day as a year, month or day by how much of the grid's date span one knob point covers (DES-123).
  Example: Given Date — Newest/Oldest First, a scroll of mixed-day content names a date at all times and the count matches that day's tile count; given a header is visible while the bar is showing, the two are indistinguishable in treatment and share a left edge; given the grid is scrolled, a hairline sits beneath the bar and beneath every drawn header, and given it rests at the top, none is drawn anywhere; given a continuous fling, the bar's date tracks the grid rather than waiting for scrolling to stop; given the grid is rubber-band scrolled past its top, no second copy of the date appears beneath the bar; given the bar appears or disappears, no animation runs; given the top edge sits between two runs, the bar names the day being left; given Filename or Size sort, neither header nor bar renders; given an asset has no capture date, it appears under a "No Date" label rather than vanishing; given enrichment moves an asset's day bucket between structural applies, no already-painted tile blanks (APP-015); given the top day's header is on screen and a newer day lands above it (an import copying oldest first), that header draws its own day and count rather than staying blank; given a day on screen gains items, its header draws the new count.; given a grid spanning ten years on a 900 pt track, the scrub label names a month; given one spanning a week, a day; given one spanning forty years on a 300 pt track, a year
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridBridge.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridStructuralApply.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridStructure.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridSupport.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridPresentation.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridLayout.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridDayLabel.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridDayBar.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridView.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogQueryGateway.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/CatalogWindowPolicy.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/CatalogModels.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridScrub.swift`

- **APP-031** The "All Imports…" surface (reached from the sidebar once more than five import batches exist) shall be a real `List(selection:)`, not `Button`s inside a plain `List`, so that Tab reaches it and Up/Down move a genuine row selection, satisfying keyboard-only operation (DES-080).
  - When the user picks a row, the surface shall navigate the sidebar to that batch; the list itself shall hold no persistent selection since picking a row replaces this view.
  Example: Given the "All Imports…" surface is shown, every batch row is reachable and selectable with the keyboard alone; given a row is picked (click or Up/Down + selection), the sidebar navigates to that batch's grid scope.

- **APP-032** When the user explicitly erases a card, Otos shall use one immutable planner-owned manifest. [verified]
  - The erase shall remove copied or preexisting media, media a library holds by content (ADR 0018) once the catalog re-confirms that holding at erase time — an available library row with the same current digest and no pending trash — useful sidecars, recognized cruft, and recognized source artifacts only after root-contained path validation.
  - A copy shall be eligible only after publication, and unknown files shall remain untouched.
  - Zero-byte media shall be excluded from copying but included in Erase.
  - The terminal erase-only bar shall report actual deleted counts, show `Card erased · N items deleted` with Eject Card, and create no import-batch selection.
  - Source deletion shall have exactly one implementation, `SourceEraser`, which every caller routes through and which refuses fixed library roots as read-only.
  - The erase sweep shall take the source root's namespace lease for the sweep alone, after the copy phase.
  - If the lease is denied, then the erase shall report an incomplete erase and never fail the import or claim the card was removed.
  - Each candidate shall be journaled before deletion, and restart recovery shall settle every journaled entry for a root under one lease.
  - The run's erase outcome shall count every source it erased, whether as that entry landed (ADR 0027) or in the end-of-run sweep; the sweep shall leave a path the run already erased alone, so an erased file is never reported as skipped.
  - Each file the erase removes, or finds already gone, shall leave the catalog as it goes: its catalog row and derived images are removed in the same durable write that clears its journal entry, so the card's catalog matches the card before it is ejected rather than after a rescan the eject usually cancels; recovery settles both together.
  - A file shall count as already gone only when its card is mounted and the path answers `ENOENT`.
  - If the card is unmounted or any other error occurs, then the erase of that file shall be a failed erase that keeps the row and the journal entry.
  Example: Given an erase-only plan, the source file is removed, its actual deletion outcome is rendered, the bar creates no import-batch selection, and unknown files remain; given a copy fails or is skipped as foreign, its source survives; given normalized-name collisions or unknown persisted volume capabilities, no row is destructively reconciled; given another owner holds the source root's namespace, the copies stand and the bar reports an incomplete erase rather than a failed import or a removed card; given the app quits mid-sweep, every journaled entry for that root is erased on the next launch; given an erase failure or skipped path, the bar reports an incomplete erase rather than a clean copy result; given a file is erased, its row leaves the catalog with its journal entry and the source's plans are marked stale.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/MediaEligibility.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/MediaImportExecutor.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/MediaImportRecovery.swift`, `OtosKit/Stores/CatalogStore.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/MediaOperationModels.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/FilesystemNamespaceSemantics+NameKey.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/ImportBarPresentation.swift`, `OtosKit/Services/MediaOperationCoordinator.swift`

- **APP-033** The media grid and the loupe/compare filmstrip shall keep the viewport anchored across a structural apply that changes what sits before it along the scroll axis: the item at the viewport's leading edge (top for the grid, left for the strip) shall stay at that edge, at the same offset. [verified]
  - Anchoring shall be withheld in exactly two cases — the grid is already at the top, where arrivals pushing content down is the signal that work is happening and nothing being read is displaced; the apply changes sidebar scope, which starts at the top.
  - A filter or sort change shall anchor as APP-042 describes, and an item it removed shall be replaced by its nearest survivor.
  - The strip shall have no top-edge exception: its viewport holds the tile the loupe is showing, so it is anchored at its leading edge too.
  - Focus changes shall still recenter the strip (APP-020); the anchor only holds the position it already has.
  - If this apply removed an anchor's item, then the anchor shall leave the scroll untouched rather than substitute another.
  - When a strip apply's restore scrolls the strip (the anchored tile changed index), the strip shall apply with no move animation, so every visible tile stays still on screen instead of sliding away and settling back.
  Example: Given a scrolled grid and an apply that inserts items above the viewport, the item at the top edge is unchanged and its on-screen offset is unchanged; given the same insert without the restore, the top item does change (negative control); given the grid at the top, no anchor is taken; given an anchor for an item no longer in the snapshot, the scroll offset is unchanged; given a strip scrolled or at its leading edge and an apply that inserts items to its left, the tile at the left edge and its offset are unchanged; given the same strip insert without the restore, the left tile changes (negative control); given a strip apply that shifts the anchored tile's index, the apply's move animation is suppressed, and given one that keeps the index or removes the anchor, it animates.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridStructuralApply.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/MediaGridAnimation.swift`

- **APP-034** Otos shall never move published import progress backwards. [verified]
  - If a progress sample would lower either the completed count or the completed bytes relative to the applied sample, then it shall be rejected as one that lost its ordering in transit, since both counters are monotonic where they are produced.
  - The import bar's remaining-time estimate shall be stated in units a person reads, with precision degrading as the estimate grows (DES-032): seconds below a minute, whole minutes below an hour, hours and minutes above.
  Example: Given a progress sample behind the applied one on either counter, it is recognised as stale and not applied, while the newer sample and a repeat of the current one are; given estimates of 45 s, 221 s and 4800 s, the bar reads `45 seconds`, `4 minutes`, and `1 hour 20 minutes` rather than a raw second count.
  Implemented in: `OtosKit/Services/MediaOperationCoordinator.swift`, `OtosKit/Models/MediaOperationModels.swift`, `OtosKit/Views/ImportBarPresentation.swift`

- **APP-035** File ▸ `Open in <Default App>` (⌘E) and File ▸ Open With ▸ shall open the current targets — the Grid's ordered selection, the Loupe's focused asset, or the active Compare pane, the same resolver curation and Move to Trash use — by handing the hand-off's files (APP-051) to LaunchServices. [verified]

[verified]

- **APP-037** Every surface that shows media — grid tiles, filmstrip tiles, the loupe pane and each compare pane — shall offer one context menu (right-click or control-click) whose contents mirror the menu bar for the item under the cursor and show no keyboard shortcuts. [verified]
  - The menu shall contain `Open in <Default App>`, Open With ▸ (apps then `Other…`), Copy (the ⌘C intent, APP-012), `Share…` (presented from the toolbar Share button, APP-036), separator, Reveal in Finder, separator, Like, Mark as Not Rated, Dislike, separator, Move to Trash.
